# Helm UX

How Helm is laid out and how a person moves through it. The product vision is in `PRODUCT.md`. The screens are drawn on the **Helm UX** canvas (a Design artifact, link added once published; its board specs are `canvas.md`); this file holds what a picture cannot: the rules behind each screen, the sitemap, the components, the journeys and the form fields.

The canvas has one clickable shell: home is the conversation with {Name}, Talk walks listening, thinking and speaking, Browse opens every view, and Settings sits behind the gear. Its Tweaks: **State** (lived in, first run), **Assistant name** (Nomos, Ellery, Bartholomew) and **Theme** (light, dark). Static boards follow, one row per journey. The older rows keep their journeys, re-drawn on the new home where they changed; round 6 adds B11 and B12 (questions in every shape), row 11, talking to {Name} (V1 to V9, N2, N3, X1, X2), and row 12, your life (P2 to P11: a pet, a place, Google Contacts, How {Name} sees you, a procedure and its run, media on a record, a key date). Round 6d redraws the reply (words first, then a compact card), adds back-to-back messages (V9), the context bar on every screen (retired in 6e), and turns Knowledge into topic documents (K1 to K8). Round 6e takes the dock off every content page: home keeps Browse · Talk · Type, and every content page ends in one built-in **ask bar** (Y1 compares three placements, Y2 is the conversation it opens, Y3 is getting around without the dock); key dates split into dates that come back and moments (E2, E9 Moments, E10 Coming up, P11).

**Round 1 of the growth area (habits, goals and what {Name} notices; Next Steps)** adds canvas rows 13 to 15 (HB1 to HB5, GO1 to GO5, LN1 to LN6) and section 1g; Board 1 is unchanged.

**Round 2 of the growth area** adds row 16, forming a habit (HF1 to HF7: the reminder offer when a habit is said, the reminder on the lock screen, a habit's stages, the reminders sheet and fading told, "this one's formed", a retired habit, and Settings' "Helm on its own"), and row 17, guardrails (GR1 to GR4: maybe hold off, pause for a while, the reasons, asked and remembered); it brings 1g to screen detail and fixes round 1 to the decision log (a no waits 4 weeks, a second 8, a third ends it; that wait is Helm's alone).

**Round 3 of the growth area** adds row 18, what matters most (IM1 to IM6: the order and its three groups, saying it, "which comes first?" when too much would share the top, moving one thing across a long list, the place on a project's page, and the order drawn at 50), and to row 16 HF8 (show a habit's name on the lock screen, with its plain warning) and HF9 (away and back: reminders keep going from Helm's server). It redraws HF2 (the lock screen now carries fixed words by default), HF5 (a carer's dose gets "Fewer reminders?" and is ended, never retired) and the guardrail reasons in GR1 to GR3 (what "comes first" means now reads the person's order), and adds the edge states of the whole area (1g, "When things go wrong").

**Owner review 1 of the growth area** redraws HB2 (saying "I took my pills" shows the habit ticked in the reply, and asks "Which one?" when two fit), HB4 (habit or repeating task, in the fixed vocabulary), LN1 and HF7 (noticing now learns from what the person says), and adds HB6 (a note on a check-in and the one "How was it?"), GO6 (the goal's why kept word for word, an offered tidy-up and a summary), LN7 ("It's felt hard lately": a different time, a smaller version or something new), HF10 (the last reminder after 3 weeks with nothing ticked) and row 19, thinking ahead (RT1 to RT4: can I go this weekend, is this the final Going Once list, Monday's call and the questions for it, what's left; LL1 to LL3: learn it later, brought back in a free slot with an overview and up to 2 videos; RA1 and RA2: research before a dated move, with the questions asked first). These boards sit on Fri, Nov 13, 2026 unless their title says otherwise.

**Owner review 3 of the growth area** redraws every question as one grouped choice (radio or checkbox rows, "Send N", "Something else" last; actions drawn apart) and every draft in full with Read it aloud (1h).

**Owner review 2 of the growth area** makes the whole area hands-free first (every question can be answered by voice, it listens after asking, it can be cut off, and "Hey {Name}" answers after listening ends), corrects row 19's wedding to a friend's that O officiates (RT1, RT3, RT4), gives RT2 Going Once's full state and RA2 a depth, and adds row 20: How {Name} sees you with sources and correct or remove (SY1), how {Name} talks (PT1), hands-free (HV1), act now or Noted (AN1), going well (WS1), where people are (LC1) and this phone's location (LC2), and how a meeting meets (MF1).

**Owner review 4 of the growth area** makes anything private (1i): a Private switch on every record's sheet or page, a quiet lock beside a private thing's name in lists, and health and care marked private by {Name} with the reason shown (D19); the lock-screen name switch is off for everything Helm can push and asks once more for a private thing (HF4, HF8). Voice follows the turn on every page: a spoken turn in the conversation over a page, or after "Hey {Name}", is answered aloud with the speaking strip and Mute, a typed one in writing (1c, Y4). LC2 is redrawn as the offer under an answer (`AbilityOfferRow`): this phone's location is never asked up front.

**Gap hunt 1 of the growth area** settles when research arrives (depth is how widely it reads, never when; 1g), adds the person's "This is medicine or care" switch on a habit and the one "Is this a medicine?" question (1i), the offer when a time away is in another time zone (1g, Where people are), scheduled actions (1j; row 22: SA1 to SA4), the habits a delete-goal or forget-person confirm names, a typed message during a spoken question, learn-later said in passing, and the owner's two answers: a lock-screen name asked for by voice is a spoken yes or no, and a wake-phrase reply on a locked phone or untouched page leaves private names out until a tap.

**Owner review 5 of the growth area** makes scheduled actions repeat like a calendar event (1j; row 23: RP1 to RP4, with the shared `RepeatPicker`), lets the person end a conversation by saying so (1c; CE1, CE2), hears the names the person has told {Name} right (1c; NM1, NM1b, NM2), and adds nicknames and family words to people (1d; NK1 to NK3b). It also names the medicine on a ticked care habit (1g, HB2).

**`{Name}` in this file and on the canvas is the assistant's name the person chose (default "Nomos").** No screen, string, constant or stored summary hard-codes "Nomos".

## 1. What is specific to Helm


| Rule | What it means on screen |
|---|---|
| Home is the conversation | Helm opens on {Name}, not on a calendar (owner, round 6: Helm runs a life, not a schedule). Home is a voice-first surface, not a chat log: at rest it shows the assistant's name, at most two quiet lines (the next fixed thing today, and "2 need you") and the big Talk button; a turn shows what you said, what {Name} says back in large type, and what it did as a card with Undo. Earlier turns fold under "Earlier today · 4" and leave home overnight; Activity keeps everything. **Home's top holds at most 3 cards at any time** (Issue 128): today Needs you and the day card (the morning card or the wind-down card, never both) and the grouping card (1l); anything more waits where it lives, never stacked above the conversation (1k). There is no capture sheet and no footer tab bar. |
| One way to talk, in one place per kind of screen (round 6e) | On home, Talk is the big button of the dock (a 68px ink button, labelled), with Type beside it and Attach while listening. On every content page the dock is gone and the page ends in the **ask bar**, one strip in the same place on every content page: "Ask about Grandpa", with the mic and the keyboard (1c). Utility screens (Settings, Connected apps, Activity, forms, Search) have neither. "Hey {Name}" works while Helm is open on screen and starts a turn about the page shown, so "move this to Friday" needs no name. No view has its own add-a-task button; Projects' "New project" and People's "Add from Google Contacts" are the only other create doors. |
| Every view is close at hand, and Browse stays calm | Home's dock is Browse · Talk · Type (owner, round 6); Browse lives on home only (round 6e). Browse pulls up one sheet of tiles sized by importance (owner, round 6b: "not all tiles need to have the same size"): "Search everything" on top; **Today** as a wide tile carrying its state ("Next: 11:00 AM · Brunch with Maya's parents", "2 events · 1 planned"); **Needs you** as a tall tile while questions wait (the verdigris dot, the count in the serif, the first question, "and 1 more"), shrinking to a thin full-width tile "Nothing waiting" when empty; **People** (three faces, "14 people · 2 pets") and **Projects** (the next deadline, "3 active"); then **small tiles** two to a row, each an icon, its count and its name: Places, Knowledge, Procedures, How {Name} sees you. Connected apps · Settings · Activity sit in a quiet foot row. **Only these four views get a big tile; a section added later is a small tile, never a big one** (the small tiles grow by rows of two; past 8 the last becomes "More · 3", opening the rest in place). A small tile shows only once its view has something. Sizes are fixed by Helm, never by the person; ordering by use is Next Steps. Every view is one tap from Browse; a content page has Back and a Home button in its app bar (the Home button is left out when Back already says "Home"), so any view is two taps from any page. No tab bar. **As built (issue 54):** the Procedures small tile always shows, empty included, with the clipboard icon (Tasks keeps the checklist icon) and no count; its empty view offers "New procedure". |
| Done by {Name}, said by {Name} | In a reply, {Name}'s words come first beside its mark (the first letter of its name in the title serif, in a tinted disc), then a compact card of what it did with one Undo (round 6d). Anywhere else, what {Name} did is one line led by the mark, in the past tense, with its Undo on the same line where it was done. What {Name} only suggests (a plan slot, a procedure, a link) has a dashed verdigris edge until the person keeps it. A dashed edge means "suggested, not yours yet" and nothing else is dashed. Verdigris means "your call": the dashed edge, the "Suggested" word and the Needs you dot, nothing else. **Round 1:** a change {Name} makes to its own plan from a pattern is told the same way, on the day it shows, with Undo; a change to something the person set is a dashed card, asked once (1g). **Round 2:** the one thing Helm ever sends first is a habit reminder the person turned on; a guardrail ("Maybe hold off", "Pause for a while?") is a dashed card like any offer and never blocks a save. **Round 3:** that reminder carries fixed words ("{Name}" · "Your evening habits are up"), never a habit's name, unless the person turns on "Show its name on the lock screen" for that one habit, where the switch says plainly that the name is then not sealed. **Owner review 1:** after 3 weeks with nothing ticked, one last reminder says so ("Your evening habit · last reminder for now") and they stop. A question {Name} asks back (Which one?, How was it?, Is this the final list?) is a reply card in the turn the person started, never a push. |
| Guessed is never dressed as known, and the world is never dressed as yours | A fact, a link or a match {Name} inferred carries a hollow ring and "{Name} inferred"; one the person said or confirmed carries a filled ring and "You said" or "You confirmed". Both rings are ink; the shape and the words carry the meaning, the same everywhere a fact appears. A note in the person's Knowledge carries the book mark (`BookMarked`), never a ring. An answer puts what comes from the person's records first, in the tinted result card with the assistant's mark; anything from general knowledge sits below a `SectionDivider` "Not from your records", in its own outlined white card led by a globe and "General knowledge", with a caution line (a health question: a bold caution first). The two never share a card. **Round 1:** a pattern {Name} noticed always carries the hollow ring and its evidence. **Owner review 1:** its evidence may be the person's own words, shown as quotes with their dates ("“too tired tonight” · Tue, Nov 3"), never paraphrased into a claim about how they feel; the goal's why is kept word for word, and a tidied version is shown only if the person chose it, the original one tap away. Anything from the web (research, a video) is marked "From the web" and sits with general knowledge, never in the records card. |
| Fixed is solid, flexible is Helm's | A Google Calendar event is a plain card with a small calendar mark and its calendar's name; it has no actions in Helm. A thing read from another app (a Waypoint flight, a Nine Lives vet visit) is the same solid card with that app's logo (`AppLogo`, 20px) in place of the calendar mark and the app's name in its meta: Helm reads it and never changes it (round 5). A planned task is a card with a round tick. A suggested task is the dashed card. Three looks, three meanings, on every day. Mail and files an answer looked up are the person's own, so they sit in the records card with a mail or file icon, never a ring. **Round 1:** a habit is a fourth look, a bare line with its emoji and a round tick (or a "+" for a count), no card: it is neither fixed nor a task. |
| No made-up numbers | No percentages, progress bars, health colours or scores. A project's state is an evidence line, at most two parts of overdue, the next dated task, quiet and a key person ("2 overdue · Final guest list in 9 days"; "No tasks yet" with none); only "overdue" takes the warning colour. A task's steps say "Steps 2 of 7"; a procedure's run says "5 of 15". **Round 1:** a habit counts this week (Monday to today) in its own unit ("11 of 13 times", "4 of 6 days"), never a streak, a chain of days or "missed"; a goal reads showing up and its next milestone ("Practised 3 of 4 this week · Chords clean in 22 days"). **Round 2:** a habit's stage is a word (Building, Holding, Formed, Building again) with no countdown to the next and no count of weeks in a row; a guardrail's reasons are counts and dates from the records ("Nothing done on Home in 12 days"), never a load score. **Round 3:** importance is an order the person said, never a flag, a star, a colour or a score; the only figure is a place in that order ("1", "2nd of 4 you put in order"), and a place is always shown with when it was said. |

**Three words, three things (round 3).** A **fact** is about the person's own people, places and projects ("Dana Ruiz is the florist"). A **note** is general knowledge the person keeps ("Cake keeps 3 days refrigerated"), in **Knowledge**; since round 6d notes feed **topics**, documents {Name} writes from them (a note in no topic is a **loose note**). A **capture** is what they told {Name}; copy calls it "what you said" (never "your note", which now means a Knowledge note).

**Four more words (round 6).** A **who** is anyone in the person's life, drawn by its kind: a person, a pet or an organization (a vet, a bakery), each with an optional photo; copy never says "who record", it says the name. A **procedure** is a reusable routine ("Packing for a trip"); a **run** is one go at it, ticked for one trip, project or event. A task stays one-off, and may have **steps**. A **topic** (round 6d, replacing "doc") is one document {Name} keeps up from the person's notes ("Car care › Washing"), nested up to three levels, with sources and history; on screen it is always a "topic", never a "doc". **Media** is a photo, screenshot, PDF, voice note or Markdown file the person sent that {Name} kept on a record.

### Look: "the household's private secretary"

**Colours are a choice (round 3).** The default is Chestnut & Sand, a calm brown; Settings has a "Colours" section (after Your assistant) with four cards, each previewing the palette in light and dark side by side: Chestnut & Sand, Plum & Pewter, Mist & Deep Teal, Porcelain & Indigo. Tapping one recolours the app at once and is remembered on every device. The description below is the original Mist & Deep Teal direction.

The owner's words: cool colours that inspire confidence, "an experienced butler for a family with lineage". Helm should feel like good stationery and a quiet, assured person who has run the house for years: cool mist paper and deep blue-teal ink (its own palette, apart from every other app's), white cards, one restrained cool accent (verdigris, the green-grey of old copper), and a serif face for titles over the site's clean sans. No nautical words, no crests in copy, no flourish. The full token table, type scale, radii, logo and mark are in `canvas.md` "Look and tokens"; they go into `helm.css` on `html.helm-theme` and into `.claude/rules/helm.md`.

- **Palette roles.** Mist page, white cards, deep blue-teal for the one filled button per screen and the Talk button; a pale teal tint for the assistant's lines, the reply card, the context chip and selected pills; verdigris only for "your call" (above). Overdue is the one warm colour (amber text); destructive is red text, never a fill except in its confirm.
- **Type.** Titles (page title, a sheet's title, the name in an identity header) are the serif at 600; everything else is the sans. Figures are tabular. Sentence case everywhere; at most one eyebrow per screen (none planned).
- **Shapes.** Corners between formal and playful: cards 14, fields 10, pills and every tick round (`TickMark`, a round checkbox rule in `helm.css`). Hairlines, no shadows on cards; the subview card keeps the site's soft shadow.
- **Tone of fixed copy.** Plain, short and assured, an exception to "Playful by default" like Runway's: past-tense reports ("Made a task and linked it to Wedding"), no cheerleading, streaks or emoji in copy. Emoji appear only where the person chose one (a project's 💍). The person's chosen tone (section 1a) changes what {Name} writes freely, not these lines (see "For the owner").

### 1a. The assistant's name and tone

- **Name.** One line of text, 1 to 16 characters, trimmed; empty means "Nomos" (the field's placeholder says so). It shows in: home's title, the wake phrase ("Hey {Name}"), every "{Name} inferred", the mark's letter, the result card's lines' accessible names, Activity's actor, empty states, Settings and the first-capture AI line. It is never written into a stored sentence: a record says *who* acted (`actor`), and the screen supplies the current name, so renaming changes every old line too. Copy is written so the name works as a subject without a possessive ("{Name} inferred", not "{Name}'s guess") and without a pronoun that assumes a gender (the assistant is "it" in help text, and help text avoids pronouns where it can).
- **Long names.** Home's serif title ends in an ellipsis before the search icon; "Hey {Name}" in hints wraps, never clips. A name the recognizer hears badly (a made-up word) still works typed; the rename field checks it (below). The mark always shows one letter (the first letter of the name, upper case); a name that starts with a digit or a symbol shows the `Feather` icon instead.
- **Tone.** Four choices, one picked: **Composed** (default: plain, complete sentences), **Warm** (friendlier, a few more words), **Brief** (the fewest words, fragments allowed), **Formal** (the butler: "Your cake tasting is this afternoon…"). Each choice shows one sample line, the same answer in that voice, using the current name. Tone shapes the sentences {Name} writes itself: answers to questions, the "why" line on a suggestion when the model writes one, and drafted steps, procedures and docs. Record lines, buttons, labels, questions ("Which Sam?") and errors read the same in every tone.
- **Where.** Settings' first group, "Your assistant": one summary row "{Name} · Composed" opening the "Your assistant" sheet (board S2). It is not asked on first run (the nurse never wants setup before the first capture); the first result card ends once with a quiet door "You can rename {Name} and choose how it sounds" → S2. Under the name field: "Say "Hey {Name}" to try it" with a mic; it answers "I heard you" or "I didn't catch that. Try a name that's easier to say, or keep typing to me." (round 6).

### 1b. Connected apps (round 5)

Helm reads from the person's other apps on this site and from Google, and changes nothing in them. One screen in Settings holds every connection.

- **Two kinds of connection, one list.** The site's own apps (A-List, Going Once, Nine Lives, Pinch, Runway, Waypoint, Worth the Wait, and any app added later) and outside services (a Google account, with Calendar, Gmail and Drive inside it). Only apps the person can open are listed; an app they can't open is hidden, never greyed. Several Google accounts are allowed; the email is the row's title, since it is what tells two apart.
- **Grants are per category.** Each app offers categories in the person's words ("Vet visits and vaccines due", not "event"). Connecting shows them with round ticks, since nothing is read until Connect; on the connection's page they become `AppToggle`s that apply at once. Turning one off takes its copy out of Helm, with Undo on the line.
- **What starts on.** Names and dates Helm can plan around (trips, plans and stays, meal plans, vet visits and vaccines due, movie nights, pickups). Off until ticked: money detail, health notes and medicines, free text the person wrote and other people's names (owner, round 5). Runway offers "Spending by category" (on, kept) and beside it "Transactions" (off, and looked up only when a question needs it, with only what an answer quotes kept, like Gmail), whose line reads "Lets Helm answer things like "what did I spend at Costco?" Looked up when you ask, never kept." (owner, round 5). As shipped (issue 30) the page reads "Read only when you ask. Only what an answer quotes is kept.": up to 5 rows an answer quotes are kept sealed with it and blanked when the connection ends, nothing else is, and the category never shows an update time, a stale line or "Update now".
- **Worth the Wait shares one category, "Box counts", which starts off (owner, round 5).** Its line: "Your boxes, how many items each holds, and how many are revealed. Never what's inside." Item contents never leave Worth the Wait, revealed or not; there is no other category to tick.
- **Kept or looked up, said on every category.** Another app's categories are kept in Helm as a sealed copy, updated every hour ("Updated today 9:30 AM"). Gmail and Drive are searched only when a question needs them, and only what an answer quotes is kept, sealed with the answer ("Searched when you ask. Only what an answer quotes is kept."); the answer says where it looked ("Looked in Gmail (you@gmail.com) just now."). Calendar stays as planned in round 2.
- **Read only, said plainly.** Every connect screen and connection page has one line: "Helm reads these and changes nothing in Waypoint." Google's screen adds that Helm can't send mail, change events or edit files.
- **Every record keeps its source.** A record from another app opens a sheet with a muted "From Waypoint" block: the app's logo, "Updated today 9:30 AM · read only in Helm", "Change it in Waypoint and Helm catches up within the hour", and "Open in Waypoint" (same tab; Back returns). A copy kept after Disconnect says "From Waypoint · no longer updated". A fact {Name} noted from an email carries a hollow ring and "{Name} inferred · from an email, Nov 5". A person {Name} met in an email shows that email in Mentions, with the account.
- **One source wins for each field, and a disagreement shows.** The calendar wins for an event's time; the app that owns a thing wins for it (Waypoint for a flight); an email never wins. When two disagree, Helm plans by the winner and says so: the card carries one line ("Two times: an email says 2:00 PM"), the sheet shows both with "Helm plans by this" on the winner, "Helm can't change your calendar" and two actions ("The calendar is right", "Remind me to ask the bakery"). A calendar event and an app's item that are the same thing (same start within 15 minutes, matching title) draw as one card with both marks. **As built (issue 33):** the calendar's mark with the other source's small logo on its corner; when the app's time differs, a muted line "A-List Tracker says 7:00 PM" on the card and in the sheet; the sheet lists each source's block, and the app's place shows when the calendar has none.
- **Trouble says what and the way out.** A Google account that stopped sharing (Google's testing limit, about weekly) shows "Stopped sharing" with the triangle icon on its row, an `InlineNotice` with "Last worked …" on its page, and Reconnect as the page's primary; answers say "Couldn't look in Gmail (you@company.com): Google stopped sharing it" with a Reconnect chip. An app that can't be reached is retried on its own and shows "Couldn't update since Thu · Try now" on its row; past a day, Today adds one muted line naming it. An app the person lost access to says "You can't open Nine Lives any more" with Disconnect. No new colour: the triangle and the words carry it, as amber stays "overdue" only.
- **Disconnect asks once.** A drawer: what stops, that nothing in the app changes, and one round tick "Also delete what came from Waypoint" naming the count ("2 trips and 14 plans. Your tasks and notes that mention them stay."), **ticked** (owner, round 5). Under the box, one muted line: "Untick to keep them, marked "no longer updated". Helm deletes kept copies after 90 days." For a Google account that only looked things up it names what {Name} noted from it, and the lines answers quoted from your mail and files, which go with it. Disconnecting Google withdraws the permission at Google too.
- **One site-wide place, as an index (design's call, light).** Grants belong to the site, not to Helm: any app may read another later, and the person needs one place to see who reads what. The account page (`/account`) gets a "Connected apps" section, shown once any grant exists, listing each reader app with what it reads and its status; a row opens that grant's page inside the reader app (Helm's L4), where it is changed. The list is never a second editor, so the two can't drift, and it costs one shared list component (`ConnectedSourcesList`, no app's words) that Helm's Settings screen uses too. Source apps get no section of their own in the MVP.
- **Gmail and Drive for others.** Their read permissions need Google's review before anyone but test users can grant them, so the Gmail and Drive ticks show only for an account Helm allows; everyone else sees Calendar alone.

### 1c. Talking to {Name} (round 6, ask bar round 6e)

- **Opening Helm is silent.** No greeting is spoken and the mic is closed until the person taps Talk or says "Hey {Name}". The wake phrase listens only while Helm is open on screen, through the site's speech layer (`useSpeechCommands`, `@/lib/speech`), never in the background; Settings → Voice turns it off.
- **A turn, in four states, one button.** Talk (idle) → **Listening** (the button rings, the words appear in large type as heard, a wave under them, Cancel and Attach beside it) → **Thinking** ("Working on it…", the button becomes Stop, a short "Got it" spoken at once in this phone's voice, and a status line naming what {Name} is looking at) → **Speaking** (the reply in large type, its words turning ink as they are said, Stop to cut it off). Listening ends after 8 seconds of quiet, a tap on the button, or "that's all". Nothing is sent while the person can still see the words being heard; Cancel throws them away. **As built (issue 12):** Talk rings while listening, thinking and speaking; Cancel takes Browse's place while listening, and Send is the Talk button's own tap; the words are in large type above the dock, a long sentence keeping its newest words in view, and the field steps aside while the voice is in use; thinking is the spinning ring and Stop, with the turn's own "Reading…" line in the thread as the one status; a turn that is sorted says its short "Got it" line in this phone's voice, and a turn that needs the person or fails says nothing yet (its written reply stands); Stop while thinking ends the wait, not the filing ("It's still being added, just not read out."); a Send with nothing heard says "Nothing came through. Tap to try again."; a turn still unsorted offline stops the wait with "Saved. It's filed once you're back online."; "undo that" takes back the latest turn filed in this session and says so ("Nothing to undo yet." when there is none, "Still working on that one. Try again in a moment." while a turn is still being read). Leaving home or closing the conversation over a page drops words being heard and cuts off a line being said; a reply that arrives on a page with no voice controls stays written. Attach, the status line under thinking, barge-in and the Stopped mark come in later issues.
- **Short spoken, whole on screen.** Spoken replies stay under about 40 words, in the chosen tone; the card under the words holds the whole answer, every record line and its Undo. "Tell me more" (said or tapped) reads on from where the voice stopped. Past 15 seconds of thinking a line joins: "Still working. You can leave; the answer waits here."
- **Barge-in.** Talking while {Name} speaks stops the voice at once and starts listening; what was cut off stays on screen, marked "Stopped · you cut in", and a tap on it reads it again.
- **Typing is always there, and a typed turn is answered in writing.** Type opens a compose bar over the dock (Attach, the field, Talk instead, Send); Enter starts a new line and Shift+Enter (or Cmd/Ctrl+Enter) sends. The field grows to 10 lines on home and 6 over a page, then scrolls, and its mic puts spoken words into the field beside any attachment, to send together. Only a spoken turn gets a spoken reply (owner, round 6). While {Name} speaks, "Mute" sits in home's header: replies stay written until Helm is closed. **As built (issue 12):** Mute sits under the line being spoken (on home and in the conversation over a page), where the eye is while the reply plays; once muted, "Muted" sits in the same place (above the dock's buttons, or beside Stop in the conversation) and a tap turns it back on. It is never saved.
- **Voice follows the turn, on every page.** Whatever page a turn starts on, it is answered the way it was asked: a spoken turn gets a spoken reply, a typed one a written reply (the owner: "If you speak to it, it speaks to you"). In the conversation over a page ("About Grandpa", opened by the ask bar's mic, by a tap on its words when the person last spoke, or by "Hey {Name}" said with the page open) a spoken turn runs home's four states inside the sheet: the words in large type as heard, "Got it" in this phone's voice, then the reply in large type turning ink as it is said, with the status `Volume2` "Speaking · natural voice" and **Mute** under the line being spoken, and Stop alone in the sheet's control row. Cutting in, "Tell me more" and the 8-second listen after a question work there as on home. A typed turn there is answered in writing only, with no status and no Mute. **Mute is one choice for the session, wherever it was tapped:** muted on home stays muted over a page and back, shown as "Muted" beside Talk, until Helm is closed (never saved). "Back to Grandpa", a swipe down or leaving the page cuts off a line being said; its words stay written in the conversation, and a reply that lands after the sheet closed stays written. **On a screen with no ask bar** (Settings, Connected apps, a form, Search), "Hey {Name}" opens the same conversation over it as "About anything" and answers aloud; a form's typed work stays under it untouched. The rule is the site's (another session is writing it into the conventions); Helm's part is that the overlay carries the speaking strip and Mute. Drawn on Y4.
- **A typed message while a spoken question is open** is its own turn, answered in writing, and doesn't close the question: its choices stay tappable and "Hey {Name}" still answers it until 2 minutes after it was asked, as after any question; then it folds as usual (a muted line, and Needs you if it had no answer).
- **Ending it by saying so (CE1, CE2).** "Never mind", "forget it", "forget about it", "cancel", "stop", "that's all", "that's it", "thanks", "I'm done", "nothing", "no thanks", "leave it" and close variants end the conversation, typed or spoken, when they are the whole turn or how it ends ("actually, never mind"); "cancel the dentist" is a request. Heard on the phone at once; something that plainly means the same ("ugh, doesn't matter") is read by the assistant and closes a moment later, the same way. **A phrase that answers the open question is its answer** ("nothing" to a pick-several is None of these; "no thanks" to a yes or no is No). **What closes:** listening stops and the microphone is let go; a question about a request not yet done, a half-made draft or a confirm waiting for a yes is dropped and nothing is written; what this conversation already did stays with its Undo. **A question about something already filed** (Which Sam? on a task already made) is not dropped: it folds to "Which Sam? · Waiting in Needs you" and the line reads "Okay, left it. It's waiting in Needs you." **What shows:** nothing is said aloud. Hands-free, one soft falling chime (the listening chime's two notes reversed, quieter), then silence. On screen the open question folds to its muted line "… · Left it"; spoken, the quiet `AssistantLine` "Okay, left it." follows the person's words, with Undo when a draft was dropped (it brings the draft back as it was, its question open again but not listened for); typed, the folded line says it alone, carrying Undo when a draft was dropped, and the compose bar closes back to the dock. With no card open, the quiet line shows in both. **"Stop" while it talks** stops the voice and, with nothing waiting, ends the conversation (the chime, no line); with a question open the voice stops and listening carries on. Said while {Name} is still working, a closing phrase stops the reply, and what the turn makes still lands with Undo. **After a close** "Hey {Name}" starts a new conversation; the dropped question can't be answered late; the 8-second quiet that already ends listening is unchanged. **"Forget it" never deletes**: forgetting needs the thing named ("forget that I like oat flat whites", answered with the struck row and Undo).
- **It hears your names (NM1, NM1b, NM2).** Each time listening starts, code hands the recognizer the names most likely this turn (the page in view, the ones named lately and most, nicknames included), never a private person or place. A heard word that is clearly closest to one known name by sound and spelling is shown as that name with a dotted underline in the live words and the sent bubble (same weight and colour, a 1.5px dotted line); a tap on it, until the reply arrives, opens a compact `Drawer`: "Heard “Shivon”", "Shown as Siobhan, a name you've told {Name}.", "Use what I said" · "Keep Siobhan" (listening carries on underneath). When two known people fit, the reply asks one pick-one question naming them ("Which Siobhan: Kelly or Doyle?"), the task already filed, never a silent pick. A correction ("no, Siobhan, S-I-O-B-H-A-N") fixes the turn and is kept on that person's names sheet under Also heard as. A misspelt name in a typed turn is matched the same way and simply used in the reply, with no underline. Settings › Voice › What the voice service hears says: "The voice provider hears what you say, and a short list of names you're likely to say (people, pets, places, projects; never a private one) so it hears them right. Never a record, a fact or a key."
- **A wake phrase on a locked phone or an untouched page** (owner). A turn started by "Hey {Name}" while the phone is locked, or while nobody has touched the page since Helm opened, answers private things without naming them, aloud and on screen: "Yes, your private task this morning is done.", "You have one private thing at 3." The labels are built by code, never by the model, and are neutral: a private task is "a private task this morning" (or afternoon or evening, by its time; "a private task" with none), never a guess at what it is (not "medicine"), and everything else private is "a private thing". The MCP tools, the already-made lines and earlier turns are masked the same way, and a private reply names nothing. Result cards use the same labels with the lock ("A private task this morning · Done 8:05 AM"). The first touch on the phone swaps the labels for the names in place, and the conversation reads as usual from then on; things that aren't private are named throughout. Drawn on Y4 (Locked phone).
- **Asking from a content page: the ask bar (round 6e, Y1 to Y3).** Home keeps its dock unchanged. Every content page (a person, pet or place, a project, a task, a topic or note, a procedure, Today, Needs you, the People and Projects lists, Knowledge, How {Name} sees you, an answer's source) drops the dock and ends in the ask bar: a full-width strip that is part of the layout, never floating, the page's content ending above it. It holds one field-like row: {Name}'s mark, the words "Ask about Grandpa" ("Ask about this topic", "Ask about today", "Ask about these 3 questions", "Ask about these 24 people", "Ask about what's coming up", "Ask about this task"; a long name is cut with an ellipsis and the chip shows it whole), then a mic and a keyboard button. The shell renders it once from the open page's context; no page places its own. **It does not move when the page scrolls**: the page scrolls above it, and it stays put at the bottom, under the thumb, the same on every content page. A tap on the words listens at once if the person last spoke and opens the field if they last typed; the mic and keyboard choose directly; "Hey {Name}" does the same as a tap. The conversation opens as a sheet over the page with the chip on top ("About Grandpa" with his face, "About this topic: Washing", "About today"); its ✕ turns it into "About anything" and that turn carries nothing from the page. "Back to Grandpa" at the sheet's top (or a swipe down) closes it, and the page comes back scrolled where it was, changed if the turn changed it. Utility screens (Settings, Connected apps, Activity, forms, Search) have no ask bar. The context is the ids of the records the page shows; the server re-reads them under the person's own account. Asking with a topic open offers "Add to this topic" on a general answer. This replaces round 6d's context bar over the dock and its two treatments (X1, X2); the other two placements (a slot in the app bar, a row under the title) stay on Y1 as Tweak options for the owner. **As built (issue 9):** Today, Tasks and an open task's sheet publish what they show and have the bar ("Ask about today", "Ask about these 9 tasks", "Ask about this task"); the bar is fixed at the page's foot, with the page padded so the last row clears it, and an open task sheet draws its own copy at its foot (one bar on screen at a time). The bar holds the mark and the words, then a mic (opens the conversation and starts listening, the field left unfocused so the keyboard doesn't cover the words) and a keyboard (opens it with the field focused), each with a 40px hit area (issue 12); a tap on the words opens the conversation with the field focused. The conversation is an overlay `Subview` over the page, opened by `?ask=page` with one history entry; its header reads "Back to Today", and the chip sits just above the field, where its ✕ turns it into "About anything" for that opening. A "this", "it" or "the fifth one" with nothing to point at files nothing and waits in Needs you. It shows the open session's turns (home shows them too) and has a draft of its own. Inbox has no bar until its records are tasks; Home and utility screens never have one.
- **Questions are asked in the conversation.** What needs the person ("Which Sam?", "Which is true now?") is asked on home, one at a time, spoken and answered by voice or a tap; "Answer later" puts it back ("Saved for later. It's waiting in Needs you."), on one row with the mic and Send under a typed answer; the mic adds spoken words to what is typed, with Cancel while it listens (owner: "Skip" didn't say it comes back). Questions come in seven shapes (1e). "2 need you" on home starts them; the Needs you view lists them for whoever prefers a list. Nothing carries a badge.
- **A reply reads as {Name} answering (round 6d).** What you said is a right-aligned ink bubble with its time under it. The reply is {Name}'s words first, beside its mark ("Done. I'll remind you tomorrow at 9, and I've noted Maya's birthday."), then a compact card of what it did, then any question. The card (`DidCard`) is indented under the words: one row per change, its icon, the record's name and one muted line ("Task · Tomorrow, 9:00 AM", "Key date · November 12 · on Maya, new"), each row a door to its record; the foot holds one Undo for the turn ("Undo all of this" when there is more than one line) and the time. After Undo the rows strike through and the card says what was taken back and what was kept (V8; the partial rule is B5's). The card never repeats the words: the words say it in a sentence, the card lists it. It replaces the round 6 log of "Made a task: …" lines.
- **Back-to-back messages make one turn while {Name} thinks (round 6d).** Bubbles sent before the reply arrives join the turn: they stack under one time, thinking says "Reading all three…", and one reply answers them all ("Got all three.") with one card and one Undo. A bubble sent after a reply starts a new turn, with its own thinking and reply. The hint under thinking says so: "Keep typing: anything you send before the reply joins this turn." Spoken turns behave the same: talking again while thinking adds to the turn; after the reply it is a new one.
- **Attach.** The paperclip (beside Talk while listening, and in the compose bar) offers: Take a photo · Choose a photo · Choose a file (PDF or Markdown, `.md`) · Record a voice note. A file shows as a thumbnail on what you said. Markdown is read as text. {Name} decides what to keep and says it in the card: "Kept the order form on Cake tasting" (a line that opens it), or "Didn't keep the screenshot" with "Keep it". A kept Markdown file is filed into a topic ("Added to Car care › Washing", 1f), kept as a loose note, or kept as media on the record it was about. Video waits for a later version (owner, round 6). **As built (issue 52, photos):** the paperclip's menu is Take a photo (only on a touch screen) · Choose photos; copy is in `HELM_COPY` (`constants.ts`). {Name}'s "reads what you send to sort it, and keeps only what's useful" note sits under the chips in the compose bar, not in the menu. Each picked photo is a thumbnail chip with a remove control, "Uploading" while it goes up and "Didn't upload" with Try again if it fails; Send waits for the uploads and says so. Up to 5 a turn ("Up to 5 photos a turn. Remove one to add another."), and only photos for now. A turn can be photos alone. A photo the assistant kept is "Kept the photo" in its reply and a line "See it on {task or fact}" that opens it ("Kept the photo" when it has no record; "Kept the photo on {task or fact}" when you kept it); a let-go photo reads "Read the photo. Didn't keep it" with "Keep it" and "Deleted in 24 hours unless you keep it.", and after 24 hours the line stays without the button. Keep it needs a connection and attaches to the first task or fact the turn changed. A let-go photo is one line, "Not kept · deleted in N hours" with Keep it. On a task or fact the kept photos sit near the top of its sheet as a row of small tiles; a tile opens a sheet with the picture (full screen on a tap), its caption, the day it was sent and Remove photo (Undo in the toast). A photo kept with no record opens the same sheet from its line. Remove photo lets the photo go for 24 hours rather than deleting it, so its line reads "You removed the photo" (or "You took the photo off {title}"), and deleting a task or fact lets go the photos kept only on it, with the delete's Undo keeping them again. **As built (issue 53, files and voice notes):** the menu is "Add a photo or file" with Take a photo (touch only) · Choose photos · Choose a PDF or Markdown file · Record a voice note. A file chip shows its icon, name and a meta line ("PDF · 2 pages", "Markdown"); a PDF over 20 pages or a Markdown file over 20,000 characters is refused when picked with a line naming the cap. Record a voice note swaps the field for a recording bar with the time, Cancel and Stop; it stops itself at 5 minutes ("Voice notes stop at 5 minutes. This one is saved."), needs a connection, and a refused microphone says how to allow it. After Stop the chip reads "Writing it out…" and the words land in the field, where the person can fix them before Send; past the day's allowance the chip says "Voice notes are used up for today. Type what it says, or try tomorrow." In the reply a kept PDF reads "Kept the PDF" with "See it on {task}"; a Markdown file "Kept {file} as a note" or "Made the topic {name} from {file}" with "See it in Knowledge: {title}" opening the note; a let-go file "Read {file}. Didn't keep it" with Keep it, and a recording "Wrote out your voice note. Didn't keep the recording" with "Keep the recording". On a record, kept files list under the photo tiles; the viewer shows a PDF's name and pages with "Open the PDF" (a new tab), a Markdown file drawn as formatted text, and a recording as a player with "What you said" and its transcript under it.
- **Offline, the phone hears and speaks (owner, round 6).** Helm bundles an opt-in offline voice: an on-device recognition model (about 60 MB, downloaded on request after a check that the phone can run it) with the site's natural or system voice for replies, so talking works offline on every phone once it is downloaded. Cutting in by voice works offline too. What you said is "Saved on this phone. {Name} will sort it once you're back online.", with the chips Helm read; questions wait under "Waiting for a connection". Before the download, or on a browser that can't run it, Talk offline shows its lock (`LockedButton`): "Download the offline voice in Settings to talk without a connection. Type for now." On screen the feature is named "Hear me offline"; its exact words are in PRODUCT.md "Speaking and Listening".
- **The mic refused.** Talk shows its lock; a tap says how to allow the microphone in this browser, and Type stays. **As built (issue 12):** a browser with no recognition or a refused microphone leaves Talk as it is; the tap opens the composer with the cursor in it and one line above the field ("Allow the microphone for this site to talk. Type for now.", or "This browser can't listen. Type for now."), which goes when the person types.
- **Voice has its own limit.** Past the day's cloud listening and speech (60 minutes and 30,000 characters a person), {Name} switches to this phone's voice, with one line saying so; the reasoning limit is A10's. **As built (issue 15):** past the speech allowance answers are read by this phone's voice with nothing said yet; the one line comes with spoken answers, and "Got it" lines are always this phone's voice.
- **`^` names a procedure or a note** in typed text (`@` a who, `#` a place, `!` urgent), as the shorthand row offers above the keyboard.

### 1d. Your life: who, places, procedures, docs and media (round 6, key dates round 6e)

- **One kind of record for anyone.** People, pets and organizations share one page (photo or initial, serif name, how they relate; tabs Facts · Tasks · Mentions · Media). A pet's page says its kind and age and offers its vet; a pet that also lives in Nine Lives offers "Connect Nine Lives" there. An organization (Greenwood Vet, Sweet Layer Bakery) is the same page with a building mark.
- **Not everyone is equal.** Each who has a tier: **Close**, **Regular** or **Passing**, set by {Name} from how often they come up and shown as "In your life · Close" (a row the person can change). The People view groups by tier under `SectionDivider`s, Close first; Passing people fold behind "Passing · 9" and stay out of pickers until they matter. In a project each linked who has a role: **key person**, **involved** or **mentioned**; the project's People tab shows key people as rows and the rest behind one door ("Involved · 6 · mentioned · 9").
- **Other names and family (NK1 to NK3b).** A person's page adds two rows to the card under the name: `Users` "Family" · "Brother · younger" (a fixed kin list and Not said · Older · Younger · Twin, the order shown as worked out from both birthdays when Helm has them; the free-text line under the name stays the person's own words) and `Tag` "Also called" · "Bubs" (the row always shows, with "Add" until a nickname exists). "Also called" opens the names sheet ("Names · Eli", also from ⋯ › Names and family): the nicknames, each with Remove, and "Add a nickname" (at most 10). "Family" opens its own sheet ("Family · Eli": the fixed list, then Not said · Older · Younger · Oldest · Youngest · Twin for a brother, sister or child, Change and Remove, with "From your birthdays: Eli is 3 years younger." under it when both birthdays have a year; a nickname another person already has is refused, naming them). Apart in the names sheet, **Also heard as** (how the name was misheard and corrected, "Ellie · You corrected it · Nov 20", each with Remove; only shown when there is one, never added by hand) arrives with Issue 105. A new name the assistant links to someone is asked once: "Is Bubs Eli?" with Yes · No · Someone new, saved only on a yes. A family word finds the person: one match is used and named in the reply ("Added for Eli: send him the photos tonight."); two ask "Which brother: Eli or Marcus?"; an order not known is asked against the person, once, and kept: "Is Eli younger than you?" (Yes · No) with one brother, the pick-several "Which of your brothers are younger than you?" with several; none known asks "Who's your brother?" as a search over the person's people (pick one, and their family word is saved) with "Add your brother", which asks for the name; the said words are never used as a name, and a spoken name works too. Nicknames and family words work typed and spoken, and private people resolve the same way on the phone and in the assistant, their names never handed to the recognizer.
  **As built (issues 34, 35 and 40):** People is a small tile in Browse and a content page (Back "Home"): Close then Regular, A to Z inside each, the person themself first with "You", a pet's species or an organization's kind under its name, then "Others" folded behind "Show others · 2" (passing ones), with `SearchInput` from 8. A who's page (Back "People") has its mark, name and kind or relation, then Facts (a quiet empty line until issue 40), Tasks (open linked tasks, rows open the task sheet) and Mentions (the turns that named them, newest first, from what is loaded). A place's page lists its tasks. A task row's detail line names its who and place ("Sam Rivera", "Home"), leaving out the one whose page lists it, and the task sheet has a Person, Pet, Organization or Place row that opens that page. A turn's card ends with a quiet line naming who and what it added or linked ("Added Jordan · Linked Miso, Acme Bakery"); Undo removes a who or place it added only while nothing else uses it, and its count names tasks only. The fold says "Others" rather than "Passing". A name that could be one of several people or places makes its task at once and the turn's card ends with the question (the prompt, each candidate as a tall choice with its relation, species or kind under it, then "Someone new" and "Don't link anyone" as quieter buttons, and Skip, after which the card says "Skipped. It's waiting in Needs you."); Needs you lists it with the prompt and opens the same question in a sheet that closes once answered. Answering needs a connection and is never queued; a spoken question is answered by saying a name, what tells them apart, "the second one", "someone new", "nobody" or "skip". A page's ⋯ holds "Change details" (a person's name, other names, kind, how they relate and contact details; a place's name, address and a "This is home" tick), "Change how close" (Close, Regular, Passing; a level set here is never changed by the assistant) and "Forget {name}", which confirms what stays and gives an Undo that puts the person back on the tasks and turns they were taken from; the person themself has no Forget. Photos and roles on a who come in later issues. When any inferred fact waits, a thin "Inferred · N" row with a hollow ring sits under the search and opens the list of them across people and places.
- **Photos for people and places.** A who's photo is picked with `PhotoPicker` (or taken from a sent photo: "Use as Grandpa's photo"). A place carries several photos as a `PhotoStrip` (4:3, "1 / 4", full screen on a tap). *As built (issue 37):* a who and a place both show their photos the same way: the main photo large (4:3) and the rest in a row of small tiles under it, each opening a sheet with Make main photo and Remove photo, full screen on a tap of the picture; the count shows in the heading from 9 photos, up to 12. "Use as …'s photo" from a sent photo waits for the media inbox.
- **Google Contacts, picked, never imported wholesale.** "Tick the people Helm should know. Only they're saved." People → "Add from Google Contacts" opens a picker of the person's Google contacts (search from the start, letter dividers, a round tick per person, "already in Helm" for ones it knows). Helm reads the list to draw the picker and saves only the ticked people, as Regular, with name, photo, email, phone and birthday. Nothing changes in Google. Google asks for the Contacts permission the first time the picker opens. **As built (issue 38):** the entry is a thin full-width "Bring in from Google Contacts" row under People's search (and a Google Contacts switch on the account's page in Connected apps), which opens the picker as an overlay `Subview` (connecting first, with the usual sheet, when no account shares contacts). Rows are a checkbox, the contact's photo or initial, the name and one email or phone, in a lazily mounted list with search; people already in People are disabled at the end of the list as "Already in People" (and the list gives way to one quiet line when everyone is in). Up to 50 can be ticked at once, the footer's one button counts them ("Bring in 3 people"), and a success toast carries Undo. A person brought in has a "From Google Contacts" card with "Update from Google" on their page; a field the person edited stays as they left it. Birthdays and companies are not brought in yet.
- **How {Name} sees you.** One page from its small tile in Browse: who you are (identity facts, "is vegan"), how you write (learned from what you write in Helm and, if the person opts in, a sample of their sent Gmail; one example line per occasion: to family, to vendors, at work), preferences (stores, brands, the gas station) and favorites (movies, restaurants). Every line carries its ring and opens its fact sheet to confirm, correct or forget; "Add" adds one in the person's words. Preferences and favorites are facts with a kind, so they appear on the who or place they're about too.
- **Topics, not docs (round 6d).** What round 6 called a living doc is now a topic: one document {Name} keeps up, nested and sourced (1f).
- **Procedures and runs.** The Procedures view (a small tile in Browse) lists the person's routines. A procedure shows its steps in groups, how often it has run, and "Start a run" (primary) with Edit. A run is ticked for one thing ("For Portland weekend · Fri, Nov 13 · from Waypoint"), shows "5 of 15", folds done groups, and takes "Just this time" steps that don't change the procedure, each with "Add to the procedure". "Finish this run" files it. {Name} suggests a procedure for an event or project with the dashed edge ("Vendor tasting · Suggested procedure · 6 steps").
- **Key dates: dates that come back, and moments (round 6e).** People, pets, organizations and the person themself keep two kinds. **Dates that come back** (Birthday, Anniversary, and a custom date with "Comes back each year" on, "Gotcha day") feed Today and Coming up. **Moments** (Moved, Started a job, Left a job, Graduated, Adopted, Met, Passed away, and a custom date with the switch off) happen once and a life holds as many as it holds. A who page's Facts tab opens with "Key dates": the dates that come back, soonest first ("Birthday · March 3, 1994 · turns 32 on Tue"; "Birthday · March 3" with a muted "Year not known · Add year"; "Anniversary · June 20 · 1 year on Jun 20"; "Gotcha day · June 4"), then a muted divider "Moments" and the latest 3 ("Started a job · Jan 2025 · Northwind", "Moved · Aug 2024 · to Denver", "Graduated · 2016"), then "See all 14 moments" (E9) and "Add a date". Empty: one "Add a key date" row. A row opens a small sheet (P11) with its source, Edit, Remove with Undo and a "Show on Today" switch (on for a date that comes back of a close or regular who; off for a moment, where on means "each year on Aug 12"). A yearly date with no day ("Birthday · June 2020") never reaches Today or Coming up; its row says so ("Day not known, so not on Today · Add day"). They come in by talking ("Noted Maya's birthday: November 12" with Undo) and from Google Contacts. How {Name} sees you lists the person's own under Who you are. Copy never guesses a pronoun and a date without its year never gets an age.
- **Moments page (E9, round 6e).** "Grandpa's moments": decade headings ("2020s") as `SectionDivider`s, year headings ("2024") inside, newest first. Rows: "Aug 12 · Moved to Denver", "Jan · Started at Northwind", "Graduated" (the year heading carries the year). A moment known only to its decade reads "Sometime in the 1980s" and goes last in its decade. The three newest decades open; older ones fold to a count ("1990s · 3") under an "Earlier" divider, each opening in place. Empty: "No moments yet. Tell {Name} about a move, a new job or another milestone."
- **Key dates on Today (round 6e).** Under Today's summary, one quiet line each for the dates that come back in the next 7 days of close and regular people, pets and the person, with "Show on Today" on: a cake mark and a chevron, "Maya turns 32 · Thu, Nov 12", "· tomorrow", "· today". At most 3: a date today or tomorrow first, then close people, then regular, soonest within each. The last row reads "+2 more this week" and opens Coming up. Each line is a door to the who's page. Passing people never show; the line is never spoken, never a push, and {Name} never brings one up on its own.
- **Coming up (E10, round 6e).** Every date that comes back, for the next 12 months, by month: reached from Today's "+2 more this week" and from a small Browse tile. Title "Coming up", subtitle "Birthdays, anniversaries and other dates that come back, for the next 12 months." Month headings ("November", then "January 2027" once the year turns); rows "Sat, Nov 14 · Maya's birthday · turns 32", "Fri, Nov 20 · Your anniversary with Sam · 5 years", "Today · Maya's birthday · turns 32"; each a door to the who's page. Close and regular people only; passing people sit behind "Show everyone". A month over 20 dates shows 20, then "Show 14 more in March". Empty: "No birthdays or anniversaries yet. Tell {Name} one, or add it on someone's page." It replaces round 6's "+N more" opening a list of the week.
- **As built (issue 44).** Right under Key dates (both above Facts) a who's page shows "Moments" with the newest three rows ("Met · Mar 2015", "Lived in Lisbon · Sometime in the 1980s") and "Add a moment" in the header; "See all 14 moments" is a full-width row with a chevron under the card (from the who's own count, shown before the moments open) and opens "Moments", grouped by decade, then year; rows under a year show only the month and day ("Jan 15", "Mar"), and the moments known only to a decade sit last in it under their own small "Sometime in the 1980s" heading, never under a year. Each row opens the key date sheet. A moment's sheet and the add form say once "Happened once. It won't show on Today."; the add form lists only the moment kinds and "Something else" (Anniversary is a date that comes back, never a moment), captions the choice "How well do you know when?" with one line under it for the choice made ("The exact day", "Just the month and year", "Just the year", "Just the decade"), and its primary reads "Add moment"; the edit title is "Edit moment". The date form lists Birthday, Anniversary and "Something else" (which comes back each year). A clash is a card "Which is true now?" with both dates; Neither opens the kept date's edit sheet under the line "Which date is right? Change it here.", and the question closes when it is saved; a read still loading shows nothing in the sheet and a failed one says "Couldn't open this date just now." (the "no longer here" line is only for a date that is gone). An answer after the kept date changed is refused ("That date changed since."). Search for moments comes with Search (issue 59).
- **As built (issue 43).** Dates that come back; moments are issue 44 (below), and "Show on Today" is left out for now (Today shows the line whenever there are dates). A who's page opens its sections with "Key dates", above Facts (`KeyDatesList`: rows like "Birthday · June 3" with "turns 32 on Tue" or "Year not known", "Add a date" opens a sheet with Birthday, Anniversary and Something else, a month, an optional day and year, and Edit and Remove with Undo toasts); a day-less date reads "Day not known, so not on Today". Today shows up to 3 lines plus "+N more this week" opening Coming up; Coming up shows 20 rows a month then "Show N more in {Month}", a search from 8 rows, and "Show everyone" to include passing people. A date for you opens your own page.
- **Media on a record.** Every who, place, project, task and event has a Media tab or list: kept photos, PDFs, voice notes and Markdown, newest first, each with where it came from ("From what you sent today 9:38 AM"). A photo opens in `ImageViewer`, a PDF in the browser, a voice note plays in place. Remove asks once and offers Undo.

### 1e. Questions in every shape (round 6b; options redrawn in owner review 3, 1h)

{Name} asks only what it can't settle itself (PRODUCT.md "Questions, In Detail"), in the reply card on home, in one of seven shapes. Each uses the site's own control, so it looks like every other form on the site:

| Shape | Control | Ends with |
|---|---|---|
| Pick one (2 to 6) | Owner review 3: radio rows in one `OptionGroup` (a fact under a name when it tells them apart); sends on tap | "Something else" (last row) → a field "Tell {Name} in your words" |
| Pick several (2 to 8) | Checkbox rows in one `OptionGroup` (staged) | "None of these", clearing the others, then "Something else"; "Send", then "Send 2"; tapped with nothing: "Pick at least one, or tell me in your own words" |
| Yes or no | Owner review 3: two radio rows, Yes then No, each with what it does | "Something else" |
| A few words | `DictationField` (a field with a mic; up to 500 characters) | Send |
| A date or a time | Owner review 3: up to three radio rows of real days | "Something else" opens `DateInput` (with time when it needs one) |
| A person, place or project | Radio rows over the person's records (search from 8) | "Someone new", "New place" or "New project", then "Something else" |
| A number | A stepper around the value with its unit ("18 people") | Save |

- **Every question can be skipped.** Skip sits at the card's foot, left. After it the card folds to one muted line: "Skipped. It's waiting in Needs you." Nothing it would change is done meanwhile; it never expires.
- **Voice first.** Spoken, one at a time, then the mic listens with no prompt word: "the first two", "both", "all of them", "none of these", "yes", a date, a number, "skip". A hint under the card says what to say. An answer that fits nothing is asked once more, then waits.
- **At most 3 on screen in a turn;** more wait in Needs you ("2 more questions in Needs you").
- **Answered and kept.** The card becomes the reply ("Got it: Sam Lee and Jordan Hayes.") and the lines it changed, with one Undo; Activity keeps "Asked: … · You picked …". Undo takes the effect back and the question waits again.
- **Who writes it.** {Name}'s questions and options are checked and capped (80 characters an option); Helm's own ("Which Sam?", "Which is true now?") keep their wording in every tone.

### 1f. Your knowledge: topics are documents (round 6d)

- **A topic reads as one document.** Its page is a `Subview`: the path above the title when it has a parent ("Car care ›"), the serif title with the person's emoji, one muted line ("Kept up by {Name} · changed Nov 7"), the latest change as a history row, "Show sources" and "Write", then the document: a short overview paragraph, headings, lists and short paragraphs, in Helm's type. Washing has sections Interior, Exterior and Ceramic coat. Nothing in it is a row of notes; the notes behind it live under each part's source.
- **Topics nest up to three levels.** A parent opens with an overview of its subtopics in a sentence or two, then one link row per subtopic ("Washing · Interior, Exterior, Ceramic coat · changed Nov 7"), then its own sections (Car care's Service). A subtopic shows its path ("Car care › Washing") and the path's parent is a link. A fourth level is refused in place: "Topics go three levels deep. Put Coating brands under Ceramic coat?"
- **Every part shows where it came from.** "Show sources" puts a small ring after each part; a tap on a part (sources shown or not) opens "Where this came from": the part quoted, then one row per source, newest first: "You said this · Nov 3, 8:14 PM" (the words quoted, "Open the conversation"), "You wrote this · Nov 9", "Saved from a general answer · Nov 5 · Not checked" ("I checked this"), "From a file you sent · Nov 9 · detailer-checklist.md" (Open), "General knowledge, added when you asked · Nov 9" ("Not from your records"); a muted "Added Nov 5 · last changed Nov 9"; footer "Remove this part" (tertiary) and "Correct this" (secondary). A part from general knowledge keeps that label wherever an answer cites it. One note can feed several topics.
- **History, with Undo.** The history row opens "History · Washing": one row per version, newest first, "Nov 9, 8:20 PM · Added 'Two buckets, one for rinsing' from what you said"; others read "You edited Exterior", "Revised when you asked: shortened Exterior", "Moved Fragrance under Car care", "Grouped under Car care", "Rearranged the sections". The newest sits in its own card with what it changed and Undo; older rows have a chevron and open that version read only with "Restore this version" (a new version). The sheet's last line: "{Name} adds to a topic when something plainly belongs, and never removes or rewrites your words unless you ask." Footer: "Tell {Name} what to change".
- **New notes come in when the topic is opened.** A topic with notes that aren't in the document yet is rewritten when the person opens it, in one batch. While it writes, a quiet muted line at the top of the document: "Bringing in 2 new notes…"; the document underneath stays readable and the line goes when the new version lands (no toast). When the day's cap of rewrites is reached: "2 new notes will be added tomorrow · Read them now", which opens those notes as a plain list. On failure: "2 new notes aren't in the document yet · Try again". None of the three is spoken.
- **Grouping is proposed, once, or done when asked.** When notes in two topics plainly share a parent, Knowledge shows one dashed suggestion card above Topics: "Group Washing and Fragrance under Car care?" with a muted reason, "Not now" and "Group them". After: "Grouped Washing and Fragrance under Car care · Undo". Moving one: "Moved Fragrance under Car care · Undo". A turn that asks for it ("put Washing and Fragrance under Car care", "make a topic of everything I've told you about the car") counts as asking: {Name} does it with the same line and Undo and asks nothing more. **As built (issue 51), departure:** the proposal is a question in the turn and in Needs you ("Group Running and Stretching under Fitness?" with "Group them", "Don't group" and Skip, which leaves it waiting), not a dashed card in Knowledge. Undo of "Group them" puts the tree back and the question waits again.
- **Saving general knowledge files itself after the first time.** "Save to my knowledge" (and "remember …" in a turn) files into the topic that plainly fits. When none fits, the first time, {Name} asks in the conversation as a pick-one question: "Where should this go?", up to 3 topics that might fit, "New topic: Washing" (the name editable in place), "Leave it loose", and Skip; spoken "Where should this go? Car care, or a new topic called Washing?" After that it files there itself, with a line and Undo: "Added to Car care › Washing: two buckets, one for rinsing · Undo · Change topic"; a saved answer reads "Saved to Car care › Washing · Undo · Change topic". Saving the same thing twice says "Already in Washing".
- **Loose notes stay** for what fits no topic: Knowledge ends with a door "Loose notes · 2"; a loose note is a drawer with its words, "Loose note · In no topic · Put it in one", where it came from and Edit. They are searched and cited like topics.
- **Disagreement and delete.** When two notes disagree the document says so in place, with both sources: "Two of your notes differ: 3 days or 5". Delete (⋯ More) confirms: "Delete Washing? Its 6 notes stay, as loose notes or in their other topics. Its subtopic Ceramic coat moves up to Car care."
- **Answers cite the version.** A records answer from a topic shows the book mark and "Your topic · Car care › Washing · Nov 9 version"; a tap opens the topic at that part.

### 1h. Questions and drafts look the same everywhere


- **A question's options are one grouped choice, never a list of actions.** Every question {Name} asks, in any row, uses the standard agentic question look (`OptionGroup`): the options in one container, each row a leading radio (single select) or checkbox (multi select), a label and a detail line. Single select sends on tap; multi select ends in "Send N". Free form is always the last row, "Something else", opening a field in place (with the mic), on single and multi alike; a question that only words can answer is the field alone. Every question offers single, multi and free form as fits: pick several where more than one can be true (which habits you took), one where only one can. "Skip" and "Not now" are a quiet text button under the group; the listening strip sits under that.
- **Actions look different.** A record to open, Copy, Undo, Read it aloud, Plan them today, Finish it in Going Once keep an icon, a chevron or a button, and never sit inside an option group or carry a radio or checkbox.
- **A draft is shown whole.** A note, a tidied why, a brief or a document {Name} drafted is a `DraftCard`: the whole text, never clipped to a line with an ellipsis; past 12 lines it fades and "Read all" opens it in full with one tap. Every draft has "Read it aloud", and "read it to me" by voice; reading stops at once on "stop", Stop, or the person talking over it, and "Read on" resumes from the sentence it stopped in.
- Applies to section 1e's shapes (redrawn on B11), HB2, HB4, HB6, GO4, GO6, LN4, LN5, LN7, HF1, HF5, GR1, GR2, IM3, RT1 (Tight), RT2, RT3 (all moments), LL1, LL3, RA1, RA2, WS1, LC2, PT1's clash, MF1 and HV1, and to B2 and B3.

### 1i. Private things


The owner asked whether the person says "this is a health habit" or simply says "keep this private". Both, as one idea: **private is a class any record can be in.** {Name} marks health and care private on its own (medicine, a dose, a treatment, someone's care, on any kind of record; the full list is PRODUCT.md "Private Things, In Detail"), and the person can mark anything private with a switch or by saying so. A private thing is never named outside Helm (a push on the lock screen, a share, a copy, an export) unless the person turns that on for that one thing. Inside Helm it reads as usual: the person's own screens show its name.

- **The Private row, on every record.** A task's, habit's, goal's, project's, note's, topic's, fact's, who's (a person, pet or organization) and procedure's sheet or page has one `PrivateRow`, the last row above the source line, after a hairline: `Lock` 18 muted, "Private", the one subtitle "Left out of copied lists, and only read as a label when you aren't looking at the screen" (the same words on the new-task sheet's row, both drawn by `PrivateSwitchLine`), and an `AppToggle`. It is immediate (a setting, not a form value): it writes the moment it is flipped, offline too, touches only its own switch, never locks the sheet and needs no toast. Turning it on never asks (it only narrows where the name goes). Making a thing private also turns its lock-screen name switch off and removes the readable copy at once, said in a muted line under the row: "Its name is hidden on your lock screen again."
- **Why, when {Name} marked it.** A thing {Name} marked shows its switch on and, under the subtitle, a 13 muted line led by a 14px `Stethoscope` (health) or `HeartHandshake` (care): "Marked private because it's about health · Change" or "Marked private because it's about someone's care · Change". "Change" (a text button with a 40px hit area) and flipping the switch off both ask once (`useActionModal().confirm`): "Make this not private?", "“Pills” is about health. Its name could then show in a share or a copy, and on your lock screen if you turn that on. {Name} won't mark it private again.", "Keep it private" · "Make it not private". After it the thing reads like any other (switch off, no reason line), {Name} never re-marks that record, and Activity keeps "You made Pills not private". A thing the person marked shows no reason line. The switch is never a `LockedButton` and never disabled: the person can always choose, and the reason line is what explains it (design-ux.md "A locked control explains itself" is for a control the person is kept out of; this one they are not).
- **Medicine or care, said by the person**. {Name} can miss a medicine it doesn't know ("start my Lexapro" reads like any habit), so a habit's page has one more immediate row, just above the Private row: `Pill` 18 muted, "This is medicine or care", subtitle "Medicine reminders don't fade, and {Name} never moves it.", an `AppToggle`. On a habit {Name} already treats as health or care it is on, with the same reason line as Private ("{Name} saw it's about health · Change"). **Turning it on** asks nothing (it only narrows): the habit loses its stage (the `HabitStage` row leaves and the identity gains "Health: {Name} never moves this, only asks."), "Fewer as it sticks" goes off ("Its reminders won't fade now." in a muted line under the row), the Private row turns on with "Marked private because you said it's medicine or care", and the habit moves under Always kept in What matters most. **Turning it off** asks once (`useActionModal().confirm`): "Not medicine or care?", "Lexapro would be treated like any habit: its reminders could fade as it holds, and {Name} could suggest a new time. It stays private unless you turn that off too.", "Keep it as medicine" · "It's not medicine". Said: "this is a medicine" (from the habit's ask bar) or "Lexapro is a medicine" answers "Got it: Lexapro is medicine. Its reminders won't fade and I won't move it." with a `DidCard` row (lock after the name, "Habit · Medicine or care · private") and Undo. Only habits carry the row.
- **"Which medicine is it?" (MC1)**. Turning "This is medicine or care" on for a habit that names no medicine asks once, as a small card under the row: "Which medicine is it?", "Its name shows on the ticked line, so a wrong tick is easy to spot.", a field "Name and dose, like metoprolol 25 mg" with Save, and `SkipQuestionButton` "It's care, not a medicine". It folds to "Medicine: metoprolol 25 mg · Change" or "Care, no medicine named · Change"; never in Needs you; unanswered, it folds when the page closes.
- **"Is this a medicine?", asked once**. When the person marks a habit private themselves and it has a dose-like count or unit ("1 tablet", "10 mg", "2 puffs"), {Name} asks once, where it was marked: as a small question card under the Private row on the habit's page, or in the reply when it was said ("Keep my Lexapro private, 1 tablet every morning"): "Is Lexapro a medicine?", single select "Yes, it's medicine or care" · "Medicine reminders don't fade, and {Name} never moves it." and "No, just private", with the `ListeningStrip` when it was said aloud. Yes turns the row above on; No leaves it as it is. Never asked again for that habit, never put in Needs you; left unanswered, it folds when the page closes.
- **Said.** "Keep the rings private", or "make this private" from a page's ask bar, answers "Buy the rings is private now. Its name won't show outside Helm." with a `DidCard` row (the lock after the name, "Task · Private now · 💍 Wedding") and Undo. "It's not private" for a {Name}-marked thing asks the same confirm as a question in the turn (single select: "Make it not private" · "Its name can show in a share or a copy", "Keep it private"). **Showing a name, asked for in a turn** (owner): "Show the name on my pill reminders", spoken or typed, never touches Private and never turns the switch on by itself. {Name} says the switch's own warning words, then asks, in the reply card: "A name shown here isn't sealed like the rest of your things. Our server keeps a readable copy to send it, it passes through Google's push service, and anyone who picks up your phone can read it. For medicine, that's the name of what you take. Show it?" (Apple's on an iPhone; the medicine sentence only for a health habit, "If the name says who it's for, that shows too." for a carer's dose), single select "Yes, show its name" · "No, keep it hidden", with the `ListeningStrip` "Listening · say “yes” or “no”" when spoken. Only a spoken or tapped yes turns it on: "Pills's name will show on your lock screen from the next reminder." with a `DidCard` row `LockOpen` "Pills · reminders · name shown" and Undo. Anything else (no, silence, a new turn) leaves it off and the question folds.
- **{Name} marks it as it is made.** When a turn makes something {Name} marks private, its `DidCard` row carries the lock after the name and nothing more is said; the record's sheet says why. A record already made that a later turn shows to be about health (a task that turns out to be a prescription) is marked then, with one `DidCard` row "Marked private · about health" and Undo.
- **The lock in lists.** A private thing's row carries a 12px lucide `Lock` in `muted-foreground` straight after its name, before any meta, with "Private" in its accessible name: Today's lines, Tasks, the People, Projects, Habits and Knowledge lists, procedures, Search results, What matters most, Coming up and `DidCard` rows. Never a colour, a badge or a chip (a list row carries none), and not on the thing's own page or sheet title (its Private row says it). It is joined to the name's last word so at 320 it never wraps onto a line alone.
