- **Outside Helm.** A push uses fixed words (HF2) unless the thing's name switch is on. **One private thing copied from its own sheet copies as usual, with no confirm:** that tap is deliberate. **A copy or export of several things** (a project's tasks, a list) leaves private things out and says so where the copy is made, in a 13 muted line with its action: "2 private things left out · Include them"; Include them puts them in for that one copy only, and the next copy leaves them out again. Nothing in Helm is shared with another person today; any later share follows the same rule.
- **Show its name on the lock screen, for everything Helm can push.** Off by default for every reminder-like thing, in that thing's Reminders sheet and nowhere else: today the habit reminders (a carer's dose included); later a task that wakes or a key date's reminder, each with the same switch and words. For a thing that isn't private, turning it on asks nothing more (its warning is always on screen, HF8). For a private thing it asks once more (`useActionModal().confirm`): "Show Pills's name on your lock screen?", "Pills is private because it's about health. Its name will be readable on this phone's lock screen, by our server and by Google's push service (Apple's on an iPhone).", "Keep it hidden" · "Show its name". Keep it hidden puts the switch back off. A private thing's Reminders sheet says so before the switch is touched: 13 muted, `Lock` 14, "Pills is private, so this asks once more."

- **As built (Issue 103).** `PrivateRow` sits on a task's sheet, a project's, person's and place's page, a topic's and procedure's page, and a note's and fact's sheet; `NewTaskSheet` carries "Keep this private" so a task is private from its first save. The lock (`PrivateTitle`, one shared piece: the title and a `size-3.5` muted lock as a `shrink-0` flex sibling, so a clamped title never clips it or leaves it alone on a line) shows in the Tasks and Planned rows, topics, projects, people, notes, facts, procedures and a turn's result rows. Tasks' menu has "Copy list": private tasks are left out, the toast says "N private things left out" with "Include them" for that copy and stays about 8 seconds; when nothing is left to copy it writes nothing and says "Nothing to copy · N private things left out" (or "The list is empty"). The inherited line is plain text with an "Open" button (40px hit area, never shrunk); turning the switch off on an inherited thing says "Still private because X is private". A cascade that only half landed says so. The lock also shows in Search rows (every group) and Coming up rows (a date is private through its person). An inherited line's Open goes to the project, person or place it comes from.

### 1j. Scheduled actions


Something {Name} does for the person at a set time without being asked each time ("every Sunday at 6, make next week's grocery list from Pinch"). It is the assistant doing work, not the person's job coming round (a repeating task) or something to form (a habit). Drawn on row 22 (SA1 to SA4).

- **Made by saying it.** "Every Sunday at 6, make next week's grocery list from Pinch" makes it with Undo and says what it will do and when: "Every Sunday at 6:00 PM I'll make next week's grocery list from Pinch's meals. The first one is Sun, Nov 22." with a `DidCard` row `CalendarClock` "Sunday grocery list · Scheduled · Sundays 6:00 PM" ›. Words that leave the day or time open get one question ("What time on Sundays?" single select 6:00 PM · 9:00 AM, then Something else). Anything a calendar can repeat can be said ("every other Tuesday at 8", "the last Friday of every month at 9", "weekdays at 7 until the end of March"); the assistant fills the rule, code checks it, and the reply says it back in the sheet's own words with the next three dates and Change: "Every 2 weeks on Tuesday at 8:00 AM · Next: Tue, Nov 24 · Tue, Dec 8 · Tue, Dec 22" (RP3). A bare hour is read as people mean it (7 to 11 morning, 12 to 6 afternoon) and always said back with AM or PM. More than once a day ("at 8 and at 6", "every 2 hours") is answered "I can run it once a day at most. Make two, one at 8:00 AM and one at 6:00 PM?", and the separate daily actions are made only on a yes. There is no "New" button: like a habit, it is said; the list's foot says how ("Ask {Name}: “every Monday at 8, list what's due on the wedding this week”.").
- **What it may do is narrow and said.** Its sheet names what it may change, in words ("May change: your tasks · Reads: Pinch's meals"), and a run never does more. Until calendar proposals (Issue 67) it changes only things inside Helm; a part that would leave Helm waits in Needs you as an approval each time ("Ready: next week's groceries on your calendar · Add it") until acting without asking (Issue 93) is on for that kind.
- **It never pushes or speaks; what it did shows on the next open.** It runs whether or not Helm is open and never sends a notification, speaks, or changes the screen the person is on. Each run leaves **one outcome line**, at the top of Today under the summary the next time the person opens it, and in Activity: an `AssistantLine` shape with `CalendarClock` in place of the mark. **Done:** "Done for you · Sun 6:00 PM: made this week's grocery list from Pinch, 14 things. · Undo" (the line opens the task). **Late** (a run that met the person's limit waits for their next open, then runs): "Done for you · Ran Mon 8:10 AM, when you opened {Name}: made this week's grocery list from Pinch, 14 things. · Undo". **Missed** (could not run within 6 hours of its time): "Sunday's grocery list was skipped: it couldn't run on time." with no button; the next run comes as usual. **Didn't run:** "Sunday's grocery list didn't run: Pinch isn't connected." with "Reconnect" (small outline, opens Pinch's connection page) · "Skip this one" (tertiary). The causes are a fixed list, each with its way on: an app not connected or no longer sharing (Reconnect), the thing it acts on is gone ("the Work project was deleted": Change it · End it). After Reconnect, a missed run whose next scheduled time hasn't come yet happens once, at once: "Pinch is back. Done for you: made this week's grocery list, 11 things. · Undo". Skip this one: "Skipped. Next one Sun, Dec 13 at 6:00 PM. · Undo" (Dec 6 is paused for Lisbon). Never amber, never a badge, never counted in "need you"; several since the last open show one line each, newest first, three at most, then "2 more in Activity". A line leaves Today at the end of the day it was first seen (a failure stays until answered or the next run); Activity keeps every one.
- **The list: Browse › Scheduled.** A small Browse tile "Scheduled · 2" once one exists. A content page (BackLink "Home", Home, ⋯, the ask bar "Ask about what's scheduled"): title "Scheduled", subtitle "What {Name} does for you at a set time. It never sends you anything; you'll see what it did next time you open {Name}." One row per action, in order of its next run, paused ones after: `CalendarClock` 18 muted, the name 15/500, one meta line, a chevron: "Next: Sun, Nov 29 · 6:00 PM", "Paused until Dec 10", "Didn't run Sun, Nov 29 · Pinch isn't connected" (ink, not amber). Ended ones fold behind "Ended · 1". A `SearchInput` from 8; at most 50, a 51st asks to end one first ("You have 50 scheduled. End one to add another."). An action whose end has come (an end date, After N times, or Does not repeat) leaves the list after its last run with one line in Activity ("Finished after its 10th run: Move morning list") and stops counting toward the 50; Ended holds only the ones the person ended.
- **Its sheet.** A row opens a `Drawer`: title serif the name; "Sundays at 6:00 PM · Pacific Time"; a muted block "What it does" with its words ("Make next week's grocery list from Pinch's meals.") and the May change line; "Next · Sun, Nov 29, 6:00 PM" (paused: "Paused until Dec 10 · skips Fri, Dec 4"); `SectionHeader` "What it did": the last three outcome lines, each opening its result, then "See all in Activity"; three `ActionRow`s: `Pause` "Pause for a while" · "Skips its runs until a day you pick" (paused: `Play` "Resume now" · "Runs again from its next time"), `PenLine` "Change when or what it does" · "The day, the time or its words", `CircleStop` (destructive) "End it" · "No more runs. What it made stays."; the `PrivateRow`; the source "You said · Mon, Nov 16, 7:52 PM". **Pause** is a small `FormSheet`: single select "Just the next run" · "Skips Sun, Nov 29"; "Until a day you pick" (a `DateInput` opens under it); "Until I say"; Cancel · Pause. **Change** is a `FormSheet` "Change Sunday grocery list": How often (`PillGroup` Every day · Every week · Every 2 weeks · Every month), Day (`PillGroup` M to S), Time, What it does (its words, a text box with the mic); the May change line updates as the words change; Cancel · Save. **End it** confirms: "End Sunday grocery list?", "No more runs. The grocery list it made stays, and Activity keeps what it did.", "Keep it" · "End it" (destructive), then "Ended Sunday grocery list. · Undo".
- **Repeats like a calendar (RP1 to RP4).** The sheet's subtitle and every line that says a repeat come from one function, `describeRepeat` ("Every week on Sunday at 6:00 PM · Pacific Time"). When this device is in another time zone than the action, each time carries the zone's short name ("8:00 AM PT"); in the same zone nothing is added. **Change** (from the sheet: the drawer closes, the `FormSheet` opens with its back control) holds "Starts with the next run. Runs already done stay as they were.", **How often** (the shared `RepeatPicker`: a field showing the rule, the words line under it with the next three dates), Time, What it does and the May change line; Save writes. A tap on How often swaps the form in place (`BackLink` "Change …") to radio rows worded from the first run being changed: Does not repeat · Every day · Every weekday (Monday to Friday) · Every week on Sunday · Every month on the 29th (months without one are skipped) · Every month on the last Sunday · Every year on Nov 29, then Custom… with a chevron; a custom rule in use is the first row. A tap picks and swaps back. **Custom…** swaps in again: Repeat every (`Stepper`, then a `SegmentedControl` Days · Weeks · Months · Years), On (weeks: seven day circles, several, the first run's day picked to start and never left with none; months: "On day 29" or "On the last Sunday", "On the fourth Monday" from a 1st to 28th, "On the last day" from a month's last day, which also adds the preset "Every month on the last day"; years: the date, no choice), Ends (Never · On a `DateInput` · After a `Stepper` and "times", 1 to 999), the words line pinned above Cancel · Done; Done takes it back to the form, a changed Back asks Keep editing / Discard. Never more than once a day, so there is no hours unit. **Coming up** replaces the Next row: the next three runs, each opening its own view in place (`BackLink` the action's name): `SkipForward` "Skip this one" · "Next one Sun, Dec 6 at 6:00 PM" (the row then reads struck through with "Skipped · Undo"), `CalendarArrowUp` "Move this one" (Day and Time for that run only, the day picker reaching at most the day before the next run; "Moved from Sun, Nov 29 · Undo" under the moved row) and `PenLine` "Change" · "This run, or this and the ones after" (the form with "Just this one" · "This and following", This and following picked; Just this one keeps only Day, Time and What it does). A skip or move after a change's first run is kept when it still falls on a run of the new repeat, otherwise dropped, and the form says which above its footer. **Pause** swaps in place in the drawer too. Said: "skip it this week", "do the groceries on Monday this time", "move it to Saturdays from now on"; ambiguous "move the grocery list to Saturday morning" asks "Just this Sunday, or every week?".
- **Said in a turn.** "What's scheduled?" (the list as a card), "skip groceries this week", "pause the errands plan until I'm back", "move the grocery list to Saturday morning", "did the grocery list run?" (answered from its last outcome), "stop the Sunday groceries" (End it's confirm as a question in the turn).
- **Away in another time zone:** runs keep home time; the away offer (1g, Where people are) can pause the ones that fall in the span.

### 1k. Your day: aims, plan and wind down (Issue 128)

One card at the top of home asks about the day, and the same card winds it down. It is a `PromptRow`-weight card in the conversation's own type, not a screen, and it never covers the dock.

- **At most 3 cards at the top of home, at any time.** Needs you ("2 need you", its first question asked in the conversation), the day card and the grouping card (1l). The day card is the morning card or the wind-down card, never both. A fourth card needs this rule changed in the same PR; until then a would-be fourth waits in its own page and its count in Browse.
- **Morning card (first open each day, after the person's day start).** Title "What do you want to get done today?" Under it the field line "Say it or type it" with the mic and keyboard, and, only when there are any, a muted "From yesterday" group above the field: one row per unfinished aim or task (its title, its length when it has one), each with Bring to today (primary) and Not today (tertiary). Said or typed, "finish the deck, call Mom, gym" files as tasks through the normal turn: the reply is "Added 3 for today: Finish the deck, Call Mom, Gym." with one Undo, and an aim that matches an existing task says "Linked to Call Mom." instead.
- **After answering.** The card shows the aims as rows, each with its length ("about 1h?" with a hollow ring for a guess, tap to fix; "30 min" once said or fixed) and its slot ("11:00 AM" or "Any time"). Above them one line says whether they fit: "These fit." or "These fit. The gym doesn't today unless you move the call." The slot's Keep, Another time and Not today are Today's. The breaks between planned tasks show as quiet gaps, never as rows.
- **Folded.** Answered: "Today: 3 aims · 2 planned" with a chevron that unfolds the card. Dismissed (an X on the card): "Skipped today's aims." with "Say what you want done". "What's my plan today?" or a tap on the folded line brings the card back open; a card put away stays folded all day.
- **Wind-down card (first open in the last 2 hours of the day).** Replaces the morning card. Title "How did today go?" and three groups with `SectionDivider`s: "Done · 2" (ticked rows, nothing to do), "Still possible tonight · 1" (the row with its length and Done), "Won't happen today · 1". Each unfinished row has three choices in one `SegmentedControl` track: Tomorrow · Later · Drop it, and under it an optional "Why?" row of chips from the fixed signals: Tired · Didn't feel like it · Too busy · Unwell · Bored · Stressed; a said reason ("too tired after the call") is read the same way and kept as the person's words. Neither is required. "Done" and "Wound down" are the card's one primary button.
- **Wound down, folded.** "Wound down: 2 done, 1 to tomorrow, 1 dropped." A tap unfolds what each aim became, with Undo on each choice.
- **Said, not tapped.** "Push the gym to tomorrow, I was tired" answers the card by voice: {Name}'s reply is the past-tense line and the card shows the choice. "I'm done for today" at the wind-down with aims left asks once "Move the 2 left to tomorrow?" (Yes · Choose each).
- **Empty, declined and offline.** No aims said and nothing from yesterday: the card is only the question. A day that ended with no aims never shows a wind-down. Offline, what is said is shown as said with "Saved here. It will be filed when you're back online." and a wind-down choice is kept and applied on reconnect. A plan that couldn't be made: "Couldn't plan today. Your aims are saved." with Try again. A rest day: "Nothing planned after a late night." under the aims.
- **Nothing is pushed.** Both cards wait for the next open; the server turns the day over and marks the wind-down ready while the app is closed, so the card is already right when it opens.
- **On the calendar.** Today and the day strip draw aims like any kept task with their length and, on a past day, ticked as built. With the one Google Calendar chosen (Issue 127), a kept aim is an event there, ticked when done, moved or removed on Tomorrow or Drop it; the aim's row never says it was written to Google.
- **Copy.** Written with `{Name}` or "the app", never the product's name; plain, short, past tense, no cheerleading, no emoji ("Added 3 for today", never "Great plan!").

### 1l. Related things: the grouping card (Issue 130)

The third top card on home. It is a dashed offer in the conversation's type, like every proposal, and it never covers the dock.

- **The card.** Title "These look like one thing: Moving". Under it the sources, one row each: the app's logo (`AppLogo`, 20px; Helm's own tasks and topics carry Helm's mark), its title ("Fall move out", "Moving costs", "Pack the kitchen") and a quiet "From Going Once" or "Your task". Beyond 4 rows, "+2 more" opens the full list in a sheet. Three actions: "Group them" (primary), "Not related" and "Not now" (tertiary).
- **Group them.** Makes the project or topic and links each source: "Grouped 5 things under Moving." with Undo. Opening Moving shows the sources and, for each, where it came from.
- **Not related.** "Won't group those again." with Undo; it stays no until something new connects them. **Not now.** The card leaves for now and may come back later ("Not now" waits, then waits longer).
- **Filed quietly.** A thing that clearly belongs to an existing project or topic is linked with no card: one next-open line, "Linked 2 things to Moving." with Undo, and one Activity line. Never spoken unless asked.
- **One at a time.** Only one grouping shows per open; the others wait. With a day card and Needs you already up, the grouping card is still the third and last.
- **Private things.** A private source shows as "A private item" with no title or app, whatever the person's other settings, unless the private-things rules allow its name.
- **Asked, not waited for.** "Anything related to my move?" is answered in the conversation at once from what is already known: "Moving has your sale Fall move out, a Horizon event and 3 tasks." Nothing here waits for the night.
- **Empty and offline.** With nothing to offer there is no card and no empty state. Offline the card still shows from what is on the phone, and the choice is kept and applied on reconnect.
- **Copy.** Written with `{Name}` or "the app", plain and short, past tense for what was done, no cheerleading, no emoji.

### 1g. Habits, goals and what {Name} notices (round 1, Next Steps; forming and guardrails round 2; what matters most, the lock screen and away round 3; what the person says, and thinking ahead, owner review 1; hands-free and how {Name} knows you)


Drawn on rows 13 to 19 of the canvas (HB1 to HB6, GO1 to GO6, LN1 to LN7; round 2: HF1 to HF7, GR1 to GR4; round 3: IM1 to IM6, HF8, HF9: HB6, GO6, LN7, HF10, RT1 to RT4, LL1 to LL3, RA1, RA2). None of it is in the MVP; everything here appears only once the person has a habit, a goal or a pattern, so a first run and the nurse's quiet Today look as before.

**The words, fixed** (decision log). A **task** is a one-off thing with an optional date ("do the shopping in two weeks"). A **repeating task** is a job on a cadence that needs doing whether or not it becomes automatic, with no stages ("clean the car every 3 weeks", "flea medicine monthly"; issue 70). A **habit** is something in a daily or weekly window the person wants to become automatic, with stages ("pills, morning and evening"; "water, 6 glasses through the day"; "guitar, 4 evenings a week"); a **health or care habit** is a habit without forming ("the cat's pill every morning", "Mom's metoprolol"). A habit is never due and never overdue, and a missed one leaves no backlog. A **procedure** is a set of steps kept once and run ("Packing for a trip"). A **scheduled action** is something {Name} does for the person at a set time ("put next week's groceries on the calendar every Sunday"; its own issue: 1j, drawn on row 22). A **goal** is a project about becoming ("Get back into guitar"): a why, milestones, and the small regular step that keeps it going (a habit or a task). "Routine" is not a kind of record: in copy it means a repeating task or a procedure, never a habit. When the words can't tell a habit from a repeating task, {Name} asks once: "Something to make a habit of, or a job to get done each Sunday?" (HB4).

- **A habit is a bare line on Today, at its time.** Today's timeline draws each habit due today as a borderless 44px line, never a card, so it reads apart from a fixed event (solid card), a planned task (card with a tick) and a suggestion (dashed card): the person's emoji (or lucide `CalendarCheck`), the name and its part of the day ("💊 Pills · morning"), the control on the left and one muted meta on the right. A habit with a window sits at its window's start ("Pills · evening" at 8:00 PM, "Practice guitar" at 7:00 PM); one that runs through the day ("💧 Water · 2 of 6", meta "Through the day") sits at the top with the all-day rows; one whose window is open now sits first in the timeline ("until 10:00 AM"). A habit not due today is not drawn. Habits never join the summary line ("2 events · 1 planned · 2 suggested" is unchanged) and Today's planner leaves a habit's length free at its time (guitar's 20 minutes), never a window without a length (pills).
- **Checked with one tap, or by saying it.** A once-a-window habit has the round tick; a counted habit has a 20px round "+" disc in the same place and its count in the name ("Water · 2 of 6"). A tap ticks or adds one and the line says so on itself with its Undo ("Done 9:45 AM · Undo", "3 of 6 · Undo"), kept until Today closes, as a task's tick does; a counted habit past its number keeps counting ("7 today"). Saying "I took my pills", "had a glass of water" or "practised guitar for half an hour" anywhere marks it, as a turn whose `DidCard` row **is the habit line as Today now shows it**: the round tick filled (or the "+" disc filled with today's count), the emoji and name, "Done 9:45 AM · 11 of 13 this week", Undo on the card (the person sees the to-do checked, not a sentence about it). The person never has to name the window: {Name} ticks the window open now; with none open, the latest one today still unticked, and says the time it marked ("Done: morning pills, marked at 11:20 AM."); the ticked line always names what was ticked, the medicine too when the habit holds one ("💊 Mom's pills: metoprolol 25 mg · morning ✓ 8:05 AM" for "Mom took her pills"), and the spoken reply names it in a few words ("Done: Mom's morning metoprolol."), unnamed on a locked phone after the wake phrase until the first touch (1c); only a window still to come asks first, as a yes or no ("Mark your evening pills as taken now, at 9:45 AM?"). **When two habits fit the words** and both are open or unticked (Pills and Vitamin D, both mornings), the reply asks one tap, "Which one?", with each habit as a choice led by an empty tick and "Both" when both are open; a habit said by name never asks. The spoken reply is a few words ("Done: morning pills."), never praise. A question ("did I take my pills this morning?") answers and changes nothing.
- **A note, and one question** (HB6). Words said with a check-in that aren't the check-in ("took my pills, felt a bit dizzy after"; "practised, felt like a chore") are kept with that day's check-in as a **note**, the person's words, sealed: a second `DidCard` row, `MessageSquareText` and the quote, "Note · You said". After a check-in {Name} may ask **one** short question in the reply card, "How was it?", with `SuggestionChips` "Good" · "Hard" · "Not into it", a few-words field with the mic, and Skip; the answer is kept as a note ("Kept that with tonight's practice."). Asked at most once a day per habit; never when the check-in already said how it went; never on a health or care habit unless the person added a note first, and then only a plain question ("Has that happened before?"), never advice; a Skip is never asked again that day and leaves nothing in Needs you. Notes show on the habit's page under "Notes" (the day, the window and the quote) and as evidence in a pattern (LN7). With Notice what works off, nothing is asked.
- **A window that passes is quiet.** An unmarked line stays on Today, muted, with "Window ended 10:00 AM", and can still be ticked ("Done 11:20 AM"); nothing turns amber (amber stays "overdue", which a habit never is), nothing says "missed", and nothing carries to tomorrow. Yesterday (C5) shows habits as ticked or not, read only.
- **Progress is "this week", in the habit's own unit.** The week runs Monday to today. A habit counts what it asks for: times for a habit with windows ("11 of 13 times"), days that reached the number for a counted one ("4 of 6 days"), times for a habit set to a number a week ("3 of 4"). Today counts once it is done; before that the week is the days already over. No streak, no chain of days, no "best week", no percentage, ring or bar. The count shows on the habit's page, in Projects for a goal, and as a muted part of the voice card's row; Today shows only today's ("2 of 6").
- **A habit's own page is small.** From the line's name on Today, the Habits tile, a goal's Habits group, Search: a content page (Back, Home, ⋯, the ask bar "Ask about Pills"). Identity (emoji disc, serif name, how often in a line, "Since Oct 2 · from what you said"); a health habit adds one muted line "Health: {Name} never moves this, only asks." Then **This week**: the count in the serif and a seven-day row, Monday first: one small dot per window under each day's letter (filled ink when marked, a hollow muted ring when not, a short muted dash for a window still to come), a counted habit's number under the letter instead (ink and bold when it reached the number), blank for a day the habit is off. Then **Earlier weeks**, one line each ("Oct 26 – Nov 1 · 13 of 14"), four, then "Show earlier". Then **Notes**, once there is one: the latest two, each the day and window over the quote ("Mon, Nov 9 · evening" / "“Hard, my fingers hurt on the F chord”"), then "Show all" once there are more. Then summary rows: each window ("Morning · 8:00 to 10:00 AM"), "Part of · 🎸 Get back into guitar" when it belongs to a goal, the source. A pattern about the habit adds one row at the end (hollow ring, "Tuesday evenings don't tend to work · Asking you") that opens it, and while it is asking, the dashed card sits under the week. ⋯ More: "Change name, emoji or times", "Pause for a while" ("Off Today until a day you pick. Paused days don't count."), "Stop this habit" ("Off Today for good. Its weeks stay."), and below a red hairline "Delete habit and its weeks" (confirm, Undo).
- **The Habits list.** A small tile in Browse once a habit exists ("Habits · 3"); a content page listing every habit, A to Z, each row the emoji, name and "11 of 13 this week" (paused: "Paused until Mon, Nov 16"); each row's meta leads with its stage ("Holding · 11 of 13 this week"; round 2); habits that formed and left Today fold behind "Formed · 1", stopped ones behind "Stopped · 1". There is no "New habit" button: a habit is said, or added from a goal's Habits group ("Add a habit"), which opens HB5's sheet empty.
- **Saying a new habit.** "Remind me to take vitamin D every morning" makes the habit at once and says where it lives: "Vitamin D is on Today each morning from 8:00 to 10:00 AM." (round 2: "remind me" also turns on its reminder, or offers it, see Forming a habit). "Morning" and "evening" default to the first and last two hours of the person's day (with day hours 8:00 AM to 10:00 PM: 8:00 to 10:00 AM and 8:00 to 10:00 PM; fixed hours, 7:00 to 10:00 AM and 6:00 to 9:00 PM, only while Your day was never set), said in the card so the person can change them. Words about the person doing something at a time or a count ("take", "drink", "practise", "stretch", "walk") make a habit; a thing to look after on a schedule ("pay rent", "water the plants every Monday", "renew") is a repeating task once issue 70 ships. When it could be either, {Name} asks a pick-one: "Should this be a habit or a task that comes back?" with "A habit · On Today each Monday, counted like 3 of 4. Never overdue." and "A task that comes back · Due each Monday. If it's missed, it waits until done."
- **A goal is a project of a second kind.** It lives in Projects under its own `SectionDivider` "Goals" (with "New goal" as its trailing action), above "Projects". Its page is a project page: identity (emoji, serif name, the **why** quoted in a line with "Why · You said · Sep 27" under it, "A goal · since Sep 27"), the evidence line, tabs **Tasks** · Milestones · People. The evidence line reads showing up and milestones, at most two parts: "Practised 3 of 4 this week · Chords clean in 22 days"; with no regular step, the next milestone and "No habit yet"; quiet, "No practice in 12 days" in muted, never amber. Tasks opens with a "Habits" divider (each habit as a row with its week count, opening the habit's page; ticks live on Today) and "Add a habit", then "Tasks" as a project's. Milestones are D3's. A goal has no "done" line and no target date by default; ⋯ More holds "Change name, why or emoji", "Mark reached" (a finished goal), "Set it aside" (its habits pause; it folds behind "Set aside · 1"), "Delete goal". **Deleting a goal, or turning it into a project** ("make guitar a project"), names its habits in the confirm and asks what happens to them, product's words: title "Delete Get back into guitar?", body "Its habit: Practice guitar.", then single select "Keep it" · "On Today with its weeks, in no goal" (ticked) and "Delete it" · "Its weeks go too", then Cancel · "Delete goal" (destructive); with several habits, product's plural shape ("Its 2 habits: …", "Keep them" · "Delete them"). Turning it into a project asks the same, with "Keep it" · "On Today, part of the project" and the button "Make it a project". A goal with no habit asks as before. After it: "Deleted Get back into guitar. Practice guitar stays on Today. · Undo". A habit that was private only because its goal was keeps that as its own mark (its Private row on, with no reason line). A goal never shows a percentage, a count of milestones as a fraction or a bar.
- **Saying a goal.** "I want to get back into piano" makes the goal with Undo, then asks one skippable few-words question: "Why does it matter to you?" The answer becomes the why, word for word ("Kept that as the why, word for word.").
- **The why, in their words** (GO6; owner: kept verbatim, an optional clean-up, a generated summary). When the why is long or loose (spoken filler, run-ons), the reply card offers once "Want it tidied up?" with the tidied text in a quote block, "Same meaning, fewer filler words. Your words stay either way.", and "Keep mine" · "Use this". A short why ("I miss it") is never offered. What the goal's page quotes is the person's own words unless they chose the tidied one ("Why · tidied from what you said · Nov 8"). A tap on the why opens a `DetailSheet` "Why it matters": the shown why, its source line, "See what I said" ("See what I wrote" for a typed why) revealing the original word for word in a muted block with "Show mine instead", and "In short", {Name}'s one-line summary (a `HelpTip`: "{Name}'s summary, used to link this goal to related things. It's never shown in place of your words."). The summary is never shown as the why and never spoken; it changes when the why changes. All three are sealed. GO5's typed why gets the same offer on its review step. Then, once, a dashed offer of a regular step ("Want a small regular step? Practise 15 minutes on two evenings a week") with Not now and "Add it". A wish said in passing ("I'd love to learn Italian someday") is not made a goal unasked: {Name} asks yes or no "Make this a goal?".

**What {Name} notices (learning).**

- **Shown with its evidence, on How {Name} sees you.** A new first section, "What I've noticed" (a `HelpTip`: "Patterns in when things get done, and in what you've said about them. {Name} quotes your words and never guesses how you feel."), shown once there is one. Each pattern is a row: the hollow ring (inferred, always), the pattern in a sentence ("Tuesday evenings don't tend to work for guitar practice"), one muted line of evidence and state ("4 of the last 5 Tuesdays · Asking you", "Asking you" in verdigris since it is the person's call). Order: asking you, then changes {Name} made, then the rest; stopped and no-longer-true patterns fold behind "Not used · 1". Three rows, then "Show all 7".
- **A pattern opens as a sheet.** Title the sentence; "What it's based on": one row per occurrence that made it, newest first ("Tue, Nov 3 · Not practised", "Tue, Oct 20 · Practised 25 min"); a "Maybe because" line only when the person's own records hold a reason ("Sprint review on your Work calendar ran to 7:00 PM on 4 of these days"), never a guess about mood, health or energy; for a pattern found in what the person said, each row is their quote with its day and what happened ("Thu, Nov 12 · “don't feel like guitar tonight” · not practised"), opening where it was said, and the sheet adds "Forget these words" ("Removes them from this pattern; your notes and the conversation stay."); the state in a muted block, in words with its date; and its actions as `ActionRow`s, each named by what it does with a subtitle for what happens next.
- **States, in the person's words.** **Asking you** (suggested); **Changed by {Name}** (acted, with the date); **Kept** (the person accepted); **Not now · not before Dec 6** (declined or undone: a first no waits 4 weeks, a second 8, said as a date; this wait is Helm's own exception, other apps' declines stay for good); **Stopped** ("won't suggest this again", after a third no or "stop suggesting this"); **No longer true** ("evenings work again": the old evidence is forgotten and noticing starts fresh).
- **Acting depends on whose the thing is.** Something {Name} itself planned (its own Today suggestions) is changed and told where it shows, as an `AssistantLine` with Undo: on Tuesday's Today, "No suggestions this evening: Tuesday evenings went unused 4 of the last 5 weeks. · Undo"; Activity keeps the line. Something the person set (a habit's days or times) is a dashed suggestion card, once, on the thing's own pages (the habit's page under its week, the goal's Habits group) and on Today on the day itself when it applies (not on a day viewed ahead): "Practise on Wednesday evenings instead of Tuesday?", the evidence in a muted line, "Not now" and the change named ("Move to Wednesday"); the reason line opens the pattern, where "Stop suggesting this" lives. A health habit or a carer's dose is never moved, only asked, with the same card in a question's words and nothing pre-chosen: "Your evening pills were marked after 10:00 PM on 4 of the last 7 days. Is 8:00 to 10:00 PM still right?" with "It's right" and "Change the time". Answering anywhere clears the card everywhere.
- **The answer is remembered, and said back.** "Move to Wednesday": "Moved Tuesday's practice to Wednesday · Undo" on the card's place. "Not now": "Okay. Not before Dec 6, and only if Tuesdays keep slipping." A card that comes back after its wait says so and shows only evidence gathered since: "You said not now on Nov 8. Since then, 4 of 5 Tuesdays went without practice." The third no: "Okay. I won't bring this up again unless you ask."
- **Said in a turn, done in a turn.** "Stop suggesting that" (the pattern on the page, or the last one shown; a Pick one when two fit), "Tuesday evenings work again", "why didn't you suggest anything for Tuesday?" (answered from the pattern with its row as the source, nothing changed). Each change is a `DidCard` row with the hollow ring and Undo.
- **Learning from what the person says** (owner: notes on check-ins, repeated "I'm tired", answers to "How was it?"; round 1's "never reads what you said" is overturned). The turn that already reads each message notes what was said about a habit or goal; a nightly pass on Helm's server reads the day's notes and those observations and finds patterns, so a pattern from words **shows the next time Helm opens**, never mid-conversation and never on home. It is shown like any pattern: hollow ring, the quotes as evidence, ask-first. Helm learns when and what, and keeps the person's words as their words: it never writes "you're tired" or "you're stressed" as a fact about them, and a health or care habit's notes are never turned into a suggestion unless the person brings it up.
- **"It's felt hard lately"** (LN7). Tiredness or reluctance about one habit said 3 or more times in 2 weeks becomes a dashed card on the habit's page (and under its line on Today on a day it's due): "Guitar practice has felt hard lately. Change something?", the reason "You said “too tired” or “don't feel like it” 3 times in 2 weeks.", "Why, and other choices" ›, then three choices as rows inside the card, each its outcome: "Make it smaller" · "10 minutes instead of 20" (done at once with Undo); "Try another time" · "Pick when; {Name} can suggest one" (the When sheet with suggested times from free time and from when it did happen); "Try something new" · "Ideas to make it fun again" (a turn with three general ideas as a pick one, marked "General ideas, not from your records"); and "Not now" in the foot ("Okay. Not before Dec 11."). Under the Helm back-off; never on a health or care habit; one suggestion card on a screen.
- **Patterns never ask for attention.** They are offers, not questions: never counted in "need you", never spoken, never on home at rest. At most one suggestion card on a screen; a second waits for the first to be answered.

**Learning has a switch (round 2).** Noticing is on from the start (owner). Settings → "Helm on its own" → "Notice what works · On" opens a small sheet: an `AppToggle` "Notice what works" with "{Name} looks at when things get done and what you say about them, to plan better and to ask about what isn't working. It quotes your words and never guesses how you feel." and a 13 muted line led by `Moon`: "To notice from your words, the day's notes and turns are read on the server overnight, and what it finds is sealed again." Turning it off asks first (`useActionModal().confirm`: "Stop noticing?", "{Name} stops changing its own plans from patterns and puts away anything waiting for your answer. What you already said yes to stays.", Keep noticing · Turn off). Off: no new patterns, no suggestion or guardrail cards, no "this one's formed" offer, no "How was it?", no nightly pass, and {Name}'s own act-and-tell changes lift (its plans go back to normal); What I've noticed keeps its rows, read only, under one muted line "Noticing is off · Turn on". A habit's stages and its fading reminders keep working: they are the habit's own rules the person turned on, not something {Name} noticed. What I've noticed ends with a thin row "Noticing · On" › that opens the same sheet, so the switch is one tap from where its results are.

**Forming a habit (round 2).** The owner: a habit system should help a person form the habit, with reminders they opt into while it builds, fewer as it holds, and a way to let it go once it has formed. Drawn on row 16 (HF1 to HF7).

- **Four stages, said as words.** **Building** (from the first day), **Holding**, **Formed**, and **Building again** (a holding or formed habit that slipped). Nothing celebrates a stage and nothing scolds one: the words are information, in muted ink, never a colour. A habit kept for someone else (Mom's metoprolol, `forWhoId` set) has no stage at all: a carer's dose is not something to form and let go. It is **ended, never retired**: its ⋯ More holds "Stop logging this" ("Off Today for good. Every dose logged stays.") in place of anything about forming, and it sits under "Stopped" like any habit given up; in place of the formed offer it gets one "Fewer reminders?" question (HF5, Tweak Habit Mom's metoprolol) once 3 weeks running have counted (Holding's rule, judged but never shown).
- **How Helm judges, said plainly.** Product's thresholds (decision log): Helm looks at whole weeks, Monday to Sunday (the week the rest of Helm counts). A week **counts** when the habit was kept every time it asked for but at most one (Pills 13 of 14, Water 6 of 7 days at its number). Three weeks that count in a row: Holding. At least 8 of the last 10 weeks counted, the last 4 among them and kept before any reminder went out, and never before 10 weeks: Formed. Two weeks in a row that don't count: Building again. Paused weeks, and days Helm wasn't opened at all, are skipped, never counted against it. The screens only ever say the rule in words, behind a `HelpTip` "How Helm judges this" beside the stage: "Helm looks at whole weeks. A week counts when you kept it every time but once at most. Three weeks in a row: holding. Most weeks for about two and a half months, lately before any reminder: formed. Two weeks that don't count: building again. Weeks you paused, and days you didn't open Helm, don't count either way. It's Helm's rule of thumb from your own ticks, not a science of habits." **No countdown** ("5 more weeks"), no count of weeks in a row and no fill between stages, anywhere: a week that doesn't count would reset a countdown, which is a streak by another name.
- **The stage on the habit's page.** Under the identity (and its health line), a `HabitStage` row: three small labelled notches, "Building · Holding · Formed", joined by a hairline; the current one a filled 10px ink dot with its word 13/600 ink, the ones behind it filled `muted-foreground`, the ones ahead hollow. One muted line under it, a date and nothing else: "Holding since Oct 26", "Building since Sep 28", "Formed since Nov 2", "Building again since Dec 14 · it held from Oct 26". The row is drawn, not a bar: the hairline never fills.
- **The stage on Today.** A habit line's right meta leads with its stage word in muted while the line is open: "Holding · until 10:00 AM", "Building · Through the day", "Formed · 8:00 to 10:00 PM". Once ticked the meta is "Done 9:45 AM · Undo" as before (the word steps aside, the line is about today). A care dose shows no word. While a reminder for that window is still to come today, a 12px `Bell` sits before the meta; it goes when the line is ticked or the reminder has gone. At 320 the meta wraps under the name as in round 1.
- **Reminders are opt-in, per habit, offered where the habit starts.** Never on by default and never asked on load (product.md "An opt-in is offered where the need starts"). When a habit is said, offered by a goal ("Add it") or added by hand, the turn's card (or the sheet's result) gets one quiet `PromptRow` under it: `BellRing` "Remind me while it builds" · "Turn on". Words that already ask for a reminder ("**remind me** to take vitamin D every morning") turn it on with the habit when this browser can already be reached: "Vitamin D is on Today each morning from 8:00 to 10:00 AM. I'll remind you at 8:00 while it builds." with a second `DidCard` row "Reminders · 8:00 AM · fewer as it sticks" (one Undo for both); when it can't, the habit is made and the row reads "Allow reminders on this phone" · "Turn on", since a browser only asks on a tap. **Turn on** opens the site's small opt-in modal (the browser's question started inside the tap): title "Reminders while Vitamin D builds"; "Helm sends a reminder at 8:00 AM each morning, unless you've already ticked it. As it sticks they get fewer, and once it's formed they stop. Change it any time on the habit's page."; for a health habit instead of the middle sentence "Reminders for medicine don't fade unless you say so."; (round 3) muted "Helm's server knows when your habit's times are and whether you ticked them, never what the habit is called. The reminder says “Time for your morning habit”."; muted "Your browser will ask first."; Not now · Turn on. Done: "Done. The first one comes tomorrow at 8:00 AM." A blocked or unsupported browser says why in the modal with the site's words and the habit stays as it is. The offer is made once per habit (no back-off: it is a setting, not a pattern); after Not now it lives only in the habit's Reminders row.
- **When a reminder comes, and how it fades.** **Building:** at the start of each window ("Morning · 8:00 AM"), or for a counted habit at times the person keeps (Water: 11:00 AM, 3:00 PM, 7:00 PM, each only while the day is short of its number). **Holding:** one, late, and only if the window is still open: 30 minutes before it ends ("9:30 PM"); a counted habit keeps only its last time. **Formed:** none. **Building again:** Building's times come back, if the person had reminders on. Never for a time already ticked, a paused habit, a day the habit is off, a window that ended, or before or after the person's day (Your day) unless the window itself sits there. Reminders due within 15 minutes of each other fold into one ("Your evening habits are up"; round 3), at most 6 a day. **They are the only thing Helm ever sends first**; "never speaks first" holds for everything else, and nothing is spoken aloud.
- **On the lock screen: fixed words by default** (owner, round 2 answer). The site's push, Helm's logo and "Helm" as the app line, opening Helm. **Title** is the assistant's name ("{Name}"); **body** one line from fixed words filled only with what Helm's server can read (the part of the day, how many, a time): one habit with a part of the day "Time for your evening habit"; two or more in one push "Your evening habits are up"; a habit with no part of the day (through the day, or two typed times) "A habit is waiting for you"; Holding's late one "Your evening habit is still open until 10:00 PM". No name, emoji, dose, person or goal, so Mom's dose on the carer's phone reads exactly like the owner's own. Never "missed", "streak", "don't break the chain", praise or a count of days. A tap opens Today on that day, scrolled to the first line the push covers, every line it covers tinted `secondary` for a second; one tap ticks each. The site's pushes carry no buttons, so there is no "Done" on the lock screen (a later step). A browser without the key goes through Opening your things first.
- **Show its name, one habit at a time** (HF8). In the habit's Reminders sheet, under the times, an `AppToggle` "Show its name on the lock screen", off for every habit (and for everything else Helm can push, 1i) and never offered (not in the opt-in modal, not in a turn's card: it is a privacy trade the person goes looking for). Asked for in a turn, {Name} says the warning and asks yes or no, and only a spoken or tapped yes turns it on (1i, "Showing a name"). Its words are always visible, not only once it is on: subtitle "Off: “Time for your evening habit”. On: “💊 Pills · evening”." and under it a 13 muted line led by a 14px `LockOpen`: "A name shown here isn't sealed like the rest of your things. Our server keeps a readable copy to send it, it passes through Google's push service (Apple's on an iPhone), and anyone who picks up your phone can read it." A health habit adds "For medicine, that's the name of what you take."; a carer's dose adds "If the name says who it's for, that shows too." Turning it on asks nothing more for a thing that isn't private (the words are already on screen); for a private thing (Pills is, being about health) it asks once more first (1i); Save applies it from the next reminder, and the Reminders summary row on the habit's page adds "· name shown". With it on, that habit's reminder reads title "💊 Pills · evening", body "Until 10:00 PM. Tap to tick it." (Holding "Still open until 10:00 PM."; counted "Water · 4 of 6 so far today." with title "💧 Water"). A push that folds a named habit with an unnamed one names only the named: "💊 Pills and 1 more evening habit". Renaming the habit changes the name from its next reminder; turning the switch off, stopping, pausing, retiring or deleting the habit, turning its reminders off and Delete all my Helm data each remove the readable copy at once (saving the switch off says so in the sheet's saved line: "Pills's name is hidden again, and its readable copy is gone."). "Show the name on my pill reminders" said in a turn opens this sheet with the switch scrolled into view rather than turning it on, so the warning is always read first.
- **Fading is told, once, where the habit is.** The day reminders thin or stop, an `AssistantLine` sits under the habit's line on Today: "Water is holding, so reminders now come only at 7:00 PM if you're short. · Keep them as they were"; "Floss is formed, so its reminders stopped. · Turn them back on". Activity keeps the line; the habit's Reminders row says the same. "Keep them as they were" turns "Fewer as it sticks" off for that habit. The fading itself is not asked: the person agreed to it on the opt-in, where it is said.
- **Health habits keep their reminders.** A health habit's reminders never fade or stop on their own; when it holds or forms, {Name} asks once, as the dashed card on its page: "Pills is holding. Want fewer reminders?", muted "Every week from Oct 5 to Nov 1 counted.", "Keep them all" (tertiary) · "Only if I'm late" (small outline), under the Helm back-off. A health habit is never offered retirement (the person can still say "stop tracking my pills"). A carer's dose gets the same question in its own words once 3 weeks running have counted: "Mom's metoprolol has been logged every day but one in each of the last 3 weeks. Fewer reminders?", muted "{Name} won't change this on its own.", "Keep them all" · "Only if it's late".
- **"This one's formed": the offer to let it go.** Once a habit (not health, not care) has been formed for a week, {Name} offers once, as the dashed `SuggestedCard` under the habit's line on Today that day and under the stage row on its page: the mark + "Suggested"; 15/500 "Floss seems to be part of your day now. Take it off Today?"; 13 muted "Kept 72 times since Aug 24, nearly every evening, mostly before any reminder."; "Why, and other choices" › (the pattern sheet, kind Formed, where "Stop suggesting this" lives); foot "Keep it on Today" (tertiary, left) · "Take it off Today" (small outline). **Take it off Today:** the card's place "Floss is off Today. Kept 72 times since Aug 24. · Undo"; a moment is added to the person's Moments; the habit moves under "Formed" in the Habits list. **Keep it on Today:** "Okay. I won't ask again before Dec 6." (a first no waits 4 weeks, a second 8, a third ends it: "Okay. I won't ask again. Say 'take Floss off Today' whenever you like."). A formed habit kept on Today stays Formed, has no reminders and can slip to Building again. Counts as the one suggestion card on its screen.
- **What a retired habit leaves behind.** A **moment** on the person, "Formed a habit · 🦷 Floss", dated the day it left Today, in Who you are's Key dates → Moments (Show on Today off), with its source "From your habit · 72 times since Aug 24". And its page, read only: identity with "Formed · off Today since Nov 8", the stage row with Formed filled, then in place of This week one serif figure "72 times" with "kept, Aug 24 to Nov 7" in muted, then Earlier weeks as before, the source row; no Reminders row, no pattern row. ⋯ More: "Bring it back to Today" ("Back on Today as Formed. Reminders stay off."), then below a red hairline "Delete habit and its weeks". In the Habits list it folds behind "Formed · 1", apart from "Stopped · 1": a habit let go after forming is not one given up. "Flossed" said afterwards changes nothing: "Floss is formed and off Today, so I didn't count it. Say 'bring Floss back' to track it again." A goal whose habit formed reads "Practice guitar formed Nov 8 · Chords clean in 22 days" in its evidence line instead of "No habit yet".
- **Reminders on the habit's page.** A summary row "Reminders" with its state, in words: "8:00 AM and 8:00 PM · stay on (health)", "11:00 AM, 3:00 PM, 7:00 PM · while it builds", "Only if it's late · 9:30 PM", "Stopped Nov 2 · formed", "Off". It opens `HabitReminderSheet` (a `FormSheet` "Reminders · Water"): an `AppToggle` "Remind me"; one time row per window (a time inside its window, 15-minute steps) or, for a counted habit, one per time with "Add a time" (3 at most); an `AppToggle` "Fewer as it sticks" · "Only a late one once it holds, none once it's formed." (on; off for a health habit, where turning it on asks nothing more); the site's device line when this browser can't be reached, with "Turn on" asking the browser in the tap; Save. A carer's dose reads "Reminders · 8:00 AM · stay on (for Mom)".
- **Settings: one place to turn them off.** A new group "Helm on its own" after Planning: "Habit reminders" · "On for 2 habits" › (a `DetailSheet` listing each habit with reminders and its state, each opening its reminder sheet, and an `AppToggle` "Pause all habit reminders" at the top, with an "Until" `DateInput` under it in round 3) and "Notice what works" · "On" ›. Whether this browser takes notifications at all stays the site's (product.md "A control lives at the scope of what it changes").
- **Said in a turn.** "Remind me about water" (on, with the browser row when needed), "stop reminding me about water" (off, Undo), "how's flossing going?" ("Formed since Nov 2. You've kept it 72 times since Aug 24."), "take water off Today" (the offer's yes or no at once, even during a wait: asked for, not suggested), "bring Floss back".

**Guardrails: pause, or maybe hold off (round 2).** The owner: Helm lets the person know when it would be wise to pause something or unwise to start it, from priority and timeline. Drawn on row 17 (GR1 to GR4). It advises and never blocks: what the person said is always made or kept first, nothing waits on the answer, and nothing is greyed or locked.

- **Two moments.** **Maybe hold off**, at the moment something new is said or made (a goal, a project, a habit, a task with a deadline). **Pause for a while?**, on something running (a project, a goal, a habit). Never about a health or care item: those are never told to pause or not start.
- **Reasons come from the records, each one checkable.** The kinds and their numbers are tech's (decision log), under design's bar: too many goals at once; a deadline that can't fit (the work before a date is more than the free time Today's planner could give it); something that **comes first** going without (nothing done on it for 14 days while other things got the time); a busy stretch ahead (the calendar's or the shifts calendar's next 14 days). {Name} speaks only with two reasons of different kinds, or one deadline that can't fit, and never in the first 7 days. **"Comes first" is the person's own order** (What matters most, below): the thing in first place (both, when two share it); with nothing said, the nearest dated deadline, and the reason says which ("Wedding comes first · you said Nov 1" or "Wedding's guest list is the nearest deadline"). **Which thing a pause names** reads the same order from the other end: never a health or care item, never what comes first, never something due within 2 weeks; of the rest, what "can wait" first, then what nobody put in order (the latest date first), then the lowest placed. A reason is a count or a date ("Nothing done on Home in 12 days", "Wedding comes first · nothing done on it since Oct 25"), never a load score, a "too much", or a guess at energy or mood.
- **Words.** "Pause", "hold off", "for a while", "until after …". Never "stop", "quit", "give up", "too much", "overloaded" or "you can't". The suggested date is the day after the reason ends (the milestone's next day, the end of the busy stretch).
- **Maybe hold off (GR1).** The reply makes the thing with Undo as always, then, before any follow-up question, the dashed card in the turn: the mark + "Suggested"; 15/500 "Maybe hold piano until after the guest list?"; up to three reason lines, 13 muted, each led by a 14px icon of its kind (`Target` a goal, `Folder` a project, `CalendarDays` the calendar, `ListOrdered` what comes first): (round 3) `ListOrdered` "Wedding comes first (you said Nov 1), and nothing on it has been done since Oct 25." · `CalendarDays` "Portland weekend, Fri to Sun, Nov 13 to 15, on your calendar."; "Why, and other choices" ›; foot "Start it now" (tertiary, left) · "Set it aside until Nov 18" (small outline). **Set it aside:** "Set piano aside until Wed, Nov 18. It comes back that morning. · Undo"; the goal sits under "Set aside · 1" in Projects with "Back Wed, Nov 18"; the why question still follows (skippable), the regular-step offer waits for its return; on Nov 18 Today carries {Name}'s line "Piano is back. · Set it aside again". **Start it now:** "Okay." and the why follows. The spoken reply names it in a few words: "Added piano as a goal. One thought on timing is on screen." A task with a deadline gets the time version only when it has a length: "Friday looks full for this", "Due Fri, Nov 13: about 3 hours already planned there, 1h 20m free before then.", "Keep Friday" · "Find a later day" (the task's When sheet).
- **Pause for a while? (GR2).** On the thing's page under its evidence line, while it asks; for a project or goal also once at the top of Projects (under the search) on the day it is first raised; for a habit on its line on Today that day. "Pause Home for a while?"; (round 3) `Flag` "Wedding's guest list tasks, due Tue, Nov 17, need about 5 hours; about 3 are free before then." · `ListOrdered` "Wedding comes first (you said Nov 1), and nothing on it has been done since Oct 25." · `Folder` "Home can wait (you said Oct 30), and nothing's been done on it in 12 days." (the last line says why Home is the one named; it is not a reason kind); foot "Keep going" (tertiary) · "Pause until Nov 18" (small outline). **Pause:** done at once with Undo, "Paused Home until Wed, Nov 18. Its tasks stay; {Name} won't plan them until then. · Undo"; Projects folds it under "Paused · 1" ("Back Wed, Nov 18"); a paused goal pauses its habits (logged decision); on the day it ends it comes back by itself, told on Today: "Home is back on your plans. · Pause again". **Keep going:** "Okay. I won't bring this up before Dec 6." A habit's version (the nurse): "Pause Stretch for this week's night shifts?", "4 night shifts, Tue to Fri, on Shifts · St. Luke's." · "Stretch happened on 1 of the last 5 such days.", "Keep going" · "Pause Tue to Fri".
- **The reasons, opened (GR3).** "Why, and other choices" opens the pattern sheet's shape: the advice as its title; "What it's based on": one row per reason, each a door to its record (Wedding's tasks due by Nov 17, Home's last done task "Fix the hall light · done Oct 27", the busy days on the calendar; round 3: "Wedding comes first · You said Nov 1" opens What matters most scrolled to Wedding, and with nothing placed the row reads "Nearest deadline"); the state block; `ActionRow`s: "Pause until a day you pick" (`DateInput`), "Keep going" ("{Name} won't raise this before Dec 6."), "Stop suggesting this" ("{Name} won't advise pausing Home again. You can still ask."). A guardrail is listed in What I've noticed like any pattern, with the hollow ring.
- **Remembered like every offer (GR4).** The Helm back-off (4 weeks, then 8, a third no ends it), coming back only on new evidence gathered since. Asked in a turn ("should I pause something?", "is it a good time to start Spanish?") it answers with the strongest guardrail even during a wait (asked, not suggested), sources as rows. "Don't tell me to pause things" asks a pick-one: "Just Home" · "Any pausing advice". At most one guardrail card on a screen and one new a day, shared with the area's suggestion cap; never in Needs you, never pushed, never spoken first, never on home at rest.

**What matters most (round 3).** The owner: "The user should be able to indicate its importance using natural language, and it's stored and used by Helm. We shouldn't necessarily restrict to 2, nor should we allow just everything to be the same high-importance." Importance is said in plain words, kept as one order (never a flag or a level), shown where the thing is, and read by the planner and by guardrails. Drawn on row 18 (IM1 to IM6).

- **What the order holds.** Everything the person is working on that competes for time: projects, goals, and habits that belong to no goal. A goal's habits take their goal's place and are never listed apart. **Health and care items are never placed** (a health habit, a carer's dose, a care project): {Name} never pauses them whatever the order, so a place would mean nothing; the order ends with one muted row "Always kept: health and care · 2" › that lists them under "{Name} never suggests pausing these, whatever the order." A **task keeps its own Importance** (Must · Should · Could, D15): "this matters most" on a task's page sets Must, on a project's page or by a project's name it moves the project. Finished projects, reached goals, and stopped or retired habits leave the order; paused and set-aside ones keep their place, marked "Paused until Wed, Nov 18", and "comes first" skips them while paused.
- **One order, three groups.** **In order**: what the person placed, numbered in a 24px gutter (13/600 tabular, muted), 1 first. Two things may share a place (both read "2"); three never do (below). **Not said yet**: everything nobody placed, by its nearest open date (a task's deadline, overdue ones from their due date, a milestone or a target date), undated ones last A to Z; the divider's trailing word "by date". **Can wait**: what the person said can wait, by date. Each group is a `SectionDivider`; an empty group is left out. No stars, levels, colours or flags anywhere.
- **Said in plain words, anywhere** (a turn on home, or the ask bar on the thing's page, where "this" means the page):

  | Said | What changes |
  |---|---|
  | "The wedding matters most", "comes first", "is my top priority" | Wedding goes to 1 on its own; what was first moves to 2 |
  | "Guitar matters more than work", "guitar before work" | Guitar goes just above Work (into In order if it wasn't) |
  | "Work matters less than guitar", "work after guitar" | Work goes just below Guitar |
  | "The wedding, then work, then guitar" | Those three, in that order, at the top, as one change |
  | "Guitar matters as much as work", "guitar too", "equally" | Guitar shares Work's place when only Work holds it; when that would make three, "Which comes first?" (below) |
  | "Guitar matters", "guitar is important" (no comparison) | Guitar goes to the end of In order, and the reply says where: "Put guitar in what matters, after Work. Say “guitar matters more than work” to move it up." A vague word never makes two things equal |
  | "Home can wait", "isn't a priority right now", "whenever" | Home goes to Can wait |
  | "Guitar doesn't matter so much now" (a step down with no place) | A pick one, "Where should guitar go?": "After Work" (the next place down) · "It can wait" · "Take it out of the order" ("Goes by its date again") |
  | "Take guitar out of the order" | Guitar goes back to Not said yet |
  | "Clear what matters" | A yes or no first, "Clear the whole order? Everything goes back to being ordered by date.", then one change with Undo |

  Each change is a row on the turn's `DidCard`: `ListOrdered` "What matters most" · "1 Wedding · 2 Guitar · 3 Work" (the first three after the change) ›, opening the order, with the turn's Undo, which puts the whole order back as it was. Spoken in a few words: "Guitar now comes after the wedding, before work." A statement that changes nothing says so ("Guitar already comes before work."). A name that fits two things asks the "From your records" pick (B11); a name that fits nothing asks "Make Spanish a goal?" (yes or no), then places it. A thing said in the same breath as it is made is placed with it ("I want to learn Italian, and it matters more than guitar"). "What matters most to me?" answers with the first three and a source row opening the order; "why is Home last?" answers "You said Home can wait on Oct 30."
- **Never inferred.** {Name} never guesses importance from how often something comes up, how much is done or how near a date is. Every place is the person's own ("You said", filled ring, with its date); there is no hollow ring in this view. Dates only order what nobody placed.
- **"Which comes first?" when too much would share the top** (IM3). Asked only when a statement would put three or more things in one place (the owner: not everything the same high importance); two sharing a place is never asked about. Before anything changes, the reply asks a pick one (B11's shape) "Which of these comes first?", with a muted line under it "The other two can share second place."; tall choices, each its emoji, name and place now ("💍 Wedding" · "First since Nov 1"; "🎸 Get back into guitar" · "2nd since 10:52 AM"; "💼 Work" · "3rd since Nov 1"); Skip. A pick puts it first and the other two share second: "Wedding comes first. Guitar and Work share second." with Undo. **Skip changes nothing**: "Okay, nothing's changed. It's waiting in Needs you." and the question sits in Needs you ("Which comes first: Wedding, guitar or work?"), counted like any question; answered there, it does the same. It is asked again only by a new statement, never on a timer. With four or more said equal, the same question, and the rest share second only when two remain; otherwise "Which comes next?" follows once.
- **What matters most, the view** (IM1, IM6). A content page: BackLink, Home, ⋯ ("Clear the order"), title "What matters most", one 13 muted line under it saying what it is for: "What comes first gets planned first, and {Name} never suggests pausing it." Then `SearchInput` from 8 rows, the three groups, the Always kept row, and the ask bar "Ask about what matters". **Its form is a list**, because its one job is an order the person can read top to bottom and move things in by name; it hides nothing and draws the same at 5 and at 50. **A row** is the place number (In order only), the emoji (or the kind's icon), the name 15/500, and the one line the person already knows from that thing's own list (Projects' evidence line, a goal's showing-up line, a habit's "Building · 4 of 6 days this week"), with "Paused until Wed, Nov 18" in its place when paused; no chips, no buttons. A row in In order carries a `GripVertical` handle (40px hit) at its right: press and drag moves it within In order, or drops it into Not said yet or Can wait; the list scrolls when the finger nears an edge. A tap anywhere else on a row opens its place sheet.
- **Its place, the sheet** (IM4, a `Drawer`). Title "Wedding's place" 22 serif; 13 muted "First · you said Nov 1" and the quote ""The wedding comes first, then work"" with its filled ring; `ActionRow`s: `ArrowUpToLine` "Make it come first" · "Everything else moves down one."; `ArrowUpDown` "Put it above…" · "Pick what it should come before." (opens a `Subview` overlay "Put Wedding above…": the In order list with search from 8, each row a tap that places it there); `Hourglass` "It can wait" · "Moves to Can wait."; `CircleSlash` "Take it out of the order" · "Goes by its date again."; and last a door row "Open Wedding" ›. A move closes the sheet and says itself where it was made, as an `AssistantLine` under the view's title, "Moved Wedding to first. · Undo", kept until the person leaves the view. This is how a thing crosses a long list in two taps; dragging is for a nearby move.
- **On the thing's own page** (IM5). A project's or goal's page gains one quiet 40px row under its evidence line: `ListOrdered` 14 and 13 muted "First in what matters · you said Nov 1" ›; a habit's page gains a summary row "What matters most" with the same words. The words by state: "First in what matters", "2nd of 3 in what matters", "Shares 2nd with Work", "Can wait · you said Oct 30", "Not said · goes by its date, Fri, Nov 20", "Not said". A goal's habit: "Through 🎸 Get back into guitar · 2nd". A health or care item: "Always kept · health" (no chevron). The row opens the order, scrolled to the thing, its row tinted `secondary` for a second.
- **Projects follows the order.** Inside each of its groups (Goals, then Projects) Projects lists placed things by place, then the rest by date, then what can wait; the evidence lines are unchanged. At the top of Projects, under the title and above Goals, a thin full-width door row (the pattern of a door to a related list): `ListOrdered` "What matters most" · "Wedding first" › ("Not said yet · by date" before anything is said; "Wedding and Work first" when two share it). Shown once two or more things could be placed. Not a Browse tile: it is Projects' companion.
- **Reviewing it, without a nag.** No timer, no "is this still right?" and nothing that fades with age; a place said months ago keeps its date. The order is mentioned on its own in one moment only: when the thing in first place is finished, reached, stopped or deleted, the line where that happened adds "Work comes first now. · Change the order" (Activity keeps it). Everything else is the person opening the view or saying it.
- **Few things, nothing said, learning off.** With fewer than two things to place there is no door and no row. With things but nothing said, the view shows Not said yet under one intro card: "Nothing said yet, so {Name} goes by dates: the nearest deadline comes first. Tell {Name} what matters most, like “the wedding matters most”." Turning learning off changes nothing here: the order is what the person said, not something {Name} noticed; only the guardrails that read it stop.

**When Helm isn't opened (round 3).** The owner: reminders keep going from the server and never lapse on their own (until 3 weeks pass with nothing ticked, below); stages keep moving. Drawn on HF9.

- **What the server reads, said where reminders are turned on.** Helm's server plans and sends each reminder from what it can read: each habit's times and days, Your day and time zone, its stage and reminder setting, and whether each time was ticked. It checks the tick just before it sends. The opt-in modal (HF1) adds one 13 muted line above "Your browser will ask first.": "Helm's server knows when your habit's times are and whether you ticked them, never what the habit is called." (a habit whose name is shown says the rest on its own switch, HF8).
- **Reminders keep going, until 3 quiet weeks.** A person who stops opening Helm keeps getting reminders for times they didn't tick, at the habit's current pace, until they turn them off or pause them (owner, round 2). Owner review 1: once 3 weeks pass with reminders going out for a habit and nothing ticked (the habit's own days with a reminder sent; paused days and days off stop the count), its next reminder is the **last one** and says so, then they stop (HF10). Fixed words: "{Name}" · "Your evening habit · last reminder for now" (with its name shown: "Stretch · evening" · "Last reminder for now. Tap to keep them coming."). Its tap opens Today with the line tinted and, under it, "Nothing's been ticked for Stretch in 3 weeks, so this is its last reminder for now. · Keep them coming". Unopened, the next Today (and the habit's page) says "Stretch's reminders stopped on Nov 29: nothing was ticked in 3 weeks. · Turn them back on", and its Reminders row reads "Stopped Nov 29 · nothing ticked in 3 weeks". Every habit, health and care too, in the same words. Nothing else changes: the habit stays on Today, and a tick, "remind me about stretching" or Turn them back on restarts reminders at its stage's pace. Never "missed" or "gave up". Round 2's "reminders stop after three days unopened" stays gone.
- **Stages keep moving.** The server judges each Monday at 03:00 in the person's zone from the week's tick flags, so a habit can reach Holding (and its reminders thin) while Helm stays closed. Days Helm wasn't opened at all count neither way, so a trip without Helm never slips a habit back; on the habit's week row such a day is blank, and the legend under the row reads "Blank: a day off, or a day you didn't open Helm."
- **Told once, on the way back.** The first Today after 3 or more days unopened, while reminders went out, starts its timeline with one `AssistantLine`: "While you were away, Nov 3 to 7, reminders kept coming. Days you didn't open Helm don't count either way." and "Pause them next time" (small outline), which opens Settings' Habit reminders sheet at "Pause all". A stage that moved meanwhile is told under its habit line as usual, dated: "Since Mon, Nov 30, Water is holding, so reminders come only at 7:00 PM if you're short. · Keep them as they were". Nothing is told when no reminder went out.
- **Pausing ahead.** Settings → Helm on its own → Habit reminders: "Pause all habit reminders" gains an "Until" `DateInput` under its switch ("Until I turn them back on" when empty). Said: "pause my habit reminders while I'm in Portland" pauses them until the day after the trip ends (read from the calendar or from Waypoint): "Paused habit reminders until Mon, Nov 16, the day after Portland. · Undo". Pausing reminders keeps the habits counting when ticked; "pause my habits while I'm away" pauses the habits (paused days don't count) and their reminders with them, and the reply says which it did.
- **Another time zone.** Reminders follow Helm's time zone (Your day), never the phone's. When Today opens on a phone set to a different zone, one line at its top: "Your phone is on Eastern time. Helm and your reminders are on Pacific. · Switch to Eastern", once per difference (Your day in Settings keeps it).

**Thinking ahead with you.** (Owner review 2: the wedding is Priya and Sam's, a friend's, which O is officiating; Monday is a video call with the couple.) The owner's cases: "can I go this weekend, and what must I do before Sunday?", a curious question saved for later, and research ahead of a dated move. Drawn on row 19 (RT1 to RT4, LL1 to LL3, RA1, RA2). The rule under all three is the owner's "central state": {Name} answers from each thing's own record, read live (the task, its steps, the calendar, Waypoint, Going Once, the email thread), and keeps no second list of its own that could fall out of step.

- **"Can I go?" is answered with a plan check** (RT1, `PlanCheckCard`). The reply's words give the verdict and its one condition ("You can, if you leave Sunday after you land from Portland at 11:00 AM. Three things first, and two to settle."); the card under them holds "Before you go · 3" (each a tick row: what, where it's from, when it fits) and "To settle · 2" (each a row with a verdigris question mark, the person's call). Its foot is one button, "Plan them today", which places the tasks at the times shown, told with Undo. When something can't fit, the verdict changes and the first thing under the words is a guardrail card, worded as a guardrail ("Maybe leave Monday, after the call with Priya and Sam?", a `Flag` deadline reason (the script promised for the call) and a `CalendarDays` reason; "Go Sunday anyway" · "Leave Monday"), under the same bar (one deadline that can't fit, or two reasons of different kinds). Habits are never listed: they travel with the person.
- **To-settle rows ask one question at a time, in the turn.** Each is a reply card (B11's shape) with tall choices named by outcome and Skip; a skipped one waits in Needs you like any question.
  - **Is this the final list?** (RT2) Going Once's sale as read just now (listed, pending and sold counts; drafts by name; and, the full state the person sees there: prices and buyers' questions) beside what the person said they'd sell and isn't listed ("the couch · You said Nov 10"): "Add the couch" (a task for today, "List the couch on Going Once", in Move) · "That's everything" (not asked again for this move) · "Finish it in Going Once" (opens the app in a new tab). {Name} never lists, edits or publishes there.
  - **Monday's call** (RT3). When a meeting is on video and the person will be near the people in it (where they'll be, LC1, against the others' homes): "Keep it on video?" with "Keep it on video" · "Meet in person" ("I'll draft a note to them. Your calendar stays as it is until they say yes.") · "Move it" (a day pick; the calendar change is a proposal, Next Steps). Meet in person makes a task and a note in the person's own voice, shown in full as a `DraftCard` with Read it aloud, Copy (`CopyTextButton`) and Edit; {Name} never sends it, and the event shows "You asked to meet in person · waiting on them" (MF1).
  - **Your questions** (RT3). The status of a task's steps that are questions: "2 are answered, 4 to ask Monday, and one says “ask Sam alone”. Do they feel ready?" · "They feel ready" · "Not yet" (finds 20 minutes today).
- **"What's left?" is always worked out fresh** (RT4). The same card with one count, "3 of 5 settled", done rows filled and muted, open rows with when they're planned, an email awaiting a reply read just then ("No reply yet · you wrote 9:02 AM"). Done rows stay until the day it was about, then the check is gone.
- **Learn it now, or later** (LL1). A question about the world with more to it than a short answer gets the short answer now (the general card, its caution) and one `PromptRow`: `GraduationCap` "Learn it properly later" · "A picture and 2 videos, when you have time" · "Save it" ("Saved to learn later. I'll bring it up when you have a free half hour. · Undo"). "Remind me to learn about X sometime" saves it directly. Never offered on a health question. **Said in passing:** "One day I want to learn how engines work" saves at once, no question: "Saved to learn later: how engines work. I'll bring it up when you have a free half hour. · Undo". When it could be a goal ("learn to fix my car's engine"), one pick-one first: "Read up on it later" · "Make it a goal you practise", then Something else. Saved things are found in Knowledge › Learn later (each row says when and where it was saved: "Nov 13 · while planning the Leavenworth drive"), in Search, and by asking ("what did I want to learn?").
- **Learn later lives in Knowledge** (LL2). A thin door row at the top of Knowledge, `GraduationCap` "Learn later · 2" ›, lists what's saved, each with when and why it was asked ("Saved Nov 13 · while planning the Leavenworth drive"), newest first, with search from 8.
- **Brought back in a free slot** (LL2). At most one a day, as a suggestion on Today in a free stretch at least 15 minutes longer than it, outside work hours and the edges of Your day, never on a day viewed ahead: the dashed card with `GraduationCap`, "AWD vs 4WD, the longer version", "30 min · you saved it Nov 13 · Suggested", and the line "Remember that thing you wanted to learn?". Not now puts it back in the list; it never expires and never nags. **After 3 Not nows** for one saved thing, its slot on Today asks instead of offering: "Still want to learn how engines work?" with "Keep it" · "Remove it" (a single-select choice card). **Lost interest** said anywhere ("I don't care about engines any more") answers "Okay, I took how engines work off your list. · Undo". **Related things meet:** when an answer touches something already saved, one line under it asks, as a single-select choice card: "You also saved how engines work. Want them together sometime?" · "Together" · "Keep them apart".
- **The overview** (LL3), a content page.
